# Vela — the plan, 2026-08-15

Built from all twelve `docs/audit/<domain>.md` documents at HEAD `92cdb06`, under the supreme rule:
**no aspect may be graded PASS unless an auditor clicked it in the running window and watched it work.**
A traced call path is `traced`, and traced grades UNVERIFIED.

## The backlog at a glance

| tag | meaning | count |
|---|---|---|
| **WIRE** | host exists, tested, no renderer reaches it | **48** |
| **FIX** | code exists and is wrong | 56 |
| **BUILD** | does not exist | 31 |
| **DOC** | prose is false at HEAD | 36 |
| | **total** | **171** |

51 items are marked blocking — a broken gate, or a lie other code is built on.

## What the supreme rule costs

**173 aspects previously graded PASS drop to UNVERIFIED**, because the evidence was a traced call path rather than a click in a running window. That number is the honest state of the product and the reason this plan exists.

## `pnpm verify` — measured at HEAD, not inherited

| gate | result | cause |
|---|---|---|
| 1 typecheck | pass | |
| **2 lint:rust** | **fail** | `'cargo' is not recognized as an internal or external command` — a PATH failure, not the shell failure the audit recorded |
| 3 test | pass | |
| **4 test:harness** | **fail** | 2 of 142: a CRLF byte comparison in `record-transcripts.test.ts`, and `ECONNRESET` on the 8 MiB cap in `server.test.ts` |
| 5 build | pass | |
| **6 test:transcripts** | **fail** | `./scripts/check-transcripts.sh` under `cmd.exe` |
| **7 test:secrets** | **fail** | same |
| 8 cargo build --locked | never runs | `&&` short-circuits at gate 2 |
| 9 cargo test --locked | never runs | same |

The chain stops at gate 2, so **five of nine links never execute on the platform Vela ships on** — including both cargo gates. The audit reported the shell problem and missed the PATH problem ahead of it.


---

## WIRE — the host exists and no user can reach it (48)

### Storage, migrations and search

#### A schedule run reaching a terminal success state  **[BLOCKING]**

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/sqlite.rs:1432 (impl), repository.rs:292 (trait), scheduler.rs:35-39 (the honest comment); src/platform/contract.ts:1598-1602 and :1664-1668 carry schedules_create/delete/list/list_runs/set_enabled and NO finisher; src-tauri/src/lib.rs:166 reap_on_boot is the only wired terminal writer`
- **Wrong** `finish_schedule_run` is implemented and covered by six tests and has zero callers outside tests — I re-derived this at HEAD with `git grep finish_schedule_run -- src-tauri/ src/`: every hit is a trait declaration, the impl, the doc comment, five scheduler.rs tests and durability.rs:413. There is no IPC command that reaches it (contract.ts's five schedules_* commands stop at set_enabled) and no renderer run loop. `vela_store::run_now` is in the same state — exported at lib.rs:88, no IPC command, no caller outside its own two tests. So ScheduleRunStatus::Succeeded is unreachable in the packaged binary: every run a user could ever see is `running` until the next launch reaps it as `failed` with "Vela stopped before this run finished".
- **Done looks like** From a running window: create a schedule due in one minute, leave the app open past its slot, then open that schedule's run history and see one row reading Succeeded with a non-zero duration and a link to the conversation it spawned. Then break it — comment out the finisher call site — relaunch, and watch the same run sit at `running` and then flip to `failed` on the next start. That requires a new IPC command reaching finish_schedule_run plus a renderer loop that drives the spawned conversation to completion.

#### Schedules and their run history survive a restart — graded PASS/reaches-user, but shipped is not-wired  **[BLOCKING]**

- **Where** `No `src/features/schedules/` exists (confirmed: src/features/ holds attachments, canvas, conversation, diagnostics, memory, models, navigation); `git grep -ln "schedules_list\|ScheduleView\|schedules_create" -- src/` returns only src/platform/contract.ts, src/platform/browser-adapter.ts and two test files; no src/data/schedules-repository.ts`
- **Wrong** The store-level durability is real and ran green, but the row's `reaches-user` grade is false at HEAD. Five schedules_* commands are registered and in contract.ts ALLOWED_COMMANDS (1664-1668), and not one module the running app loads invokes any of them. A user cannot create, see, enable or inspect a schedule, so nothing a user does can produce the run history this aspect proves survives a restart. Its correct shipped grade is not-wired, which is why it appears here despite the PASS.
- **Done looks like** From an open conversation, click Schedules in the sidebar, create a daily schedule with a title and a prompt, see it appear in the list with its next-run time, quit Vela, relaunch, and see the same schedule still listed with its history intact. Break test: disable the schedule from that surface and confirm it stops appearing as due. Requires a src/data/schedules-repository.ts on the import graph and a React surface mounted from App.tsx — neither exists.

#### Default project seeded by a migration — graded PASS/reaches-user, but nothing a user does can reference the seeded row

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/migrations/0005_project_workspace.sql:48+ (the seed); src/platform/contract.ts:939-942 — StoreCreateConversationReq carries only `title`, no projectId; src-tauri/src/ipc/store.rs:342 `create_conversation(NewConversation::titled(title))`; src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/model.rs:607-620 leaves project_id: None; migrations/0001_initial_schema.sql:29 `project_id TEXT REFERENCES projects (id) ON DELETE SET NULL` (nullable)`
- **Wrong** The seed migration runs at every launch, so the row exists — but I re-derived that no conversation a user creates can ever point at it. StoreCreateConversationReq has no projectId field, ipc/store.rs:342 constructs NewConversation::titled which leaves project_id None, and the column is nullable. Combined with the Projects domain's finding that no project surface exists (App.tsx:93 states it outright), every conversation on a real user's disk has project_id = NULL. The aspect's shipped grade should be not-wired, not reaches-user.
- **Done looks like** Create a conversation from the running UI, then run `SELECT id, project_id FROM conversations ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 1` against %APPDATA%\dev.vela.desktop\vela.db and see the seeded default project id rather than NULL — and see that project named somewhere in the window. Requires projectId on StoreCreateConversationReq, a call site that passes it, and a project surface. Break test: delete the seed from a copy of the database and confirm the app reports it rather than silently creating orphan conversations.


### Projects, skills and MCP

#### The private agent workspace is scoped to a run

- **Where** `src/platform/contract-sandbox.ts:880 projectFilesystemScope; src/platform/project-run-scope.test.ts:31 (its own header admits the absence), :140 and :226 (its only two call sites); src/runtime/app-runtime.ts:75-83; src-tauri/src/ipc/project.rs is the only file under src-tauri/src that names workspace_path / ProjectPaths / vela_projects`
- **Wrong** projectFilesystemScope has exactly one caller in the whole tree and it is its own test file — re-derived at HEAD with `git grep -n projectFilesystemScope -- src/`: every other hit is a doc comment. The private workspace is created at project_create and repaired at every project_layout, then read by nothing: no ToolExecutor builds a FilesystemScope from a ProjectLayout, so no run in this application is scoped by the three-mount rule. The seven tests in project-run-scope.test.ts hold a rule for a function no production path reaches.
- **Done looks like** In the running window, with a project open that has a working directory set: ask the agent to write a file and watch it land under that project's private workspace (verified in Explorer, not in a fixture); then ask it to write outside the three mounts and see the scope's own refusal rendered in the transcript. Break it: flip the workspace mount to read-only in projectFilesystemScope and watch a run that previously wrote now fail in the window.

#### Project instructions reach a model turn  **[BLOCKING]**

- **Where** `src/runtime/app-runtime.ts:83 `readProjectInstructions: () => Promise.resolve(null)`, justified by the false comment at :18-24; src/runtime/project-context.ts:12-14; src-tauri/src/ipc/chat.rs:111 (ChatSendReq carries no projectId)`
- **Wrong** The composition root hardcodes the reader to null for every project, and the stated reason — 'COMMAND_ALLOWLIST in src/platform/contract.ts has no command that reads a project, so there is nothing to call' — is false: project_get is at contract.ts:1652 and registered at lib.rs:204. createProjectContextResolver therefore indexes nothing, every caller's preload comes back empty, and no run is degraded for material it was never promised. chat_send is not a second route: ChatSendReq is { turnId, providerId, modelId, messages, tools, toolChoice }.
- **Done looks like** In the running window: set a project's instructions to a sentence containing a distinctive token, start an agent run inside that project, and see the token in the run's system input (via the debug log or the context meter). Switch to a second project and confirm the first project's instructions are absent from its runs. Then break it: restore `() => Promise.resolve(null)` and watch a named test that exercises the real createAgentRuntime go red — note that agent-run.test.tsx:86-87 and adapter-integration.test.ts:63 supply their own reader, so no existing test can catch this.

But nothing on the import graph from src/main.tsx issues any of the eight commands, so the whole layer is unreachable. This cannot be PASS under the supreme rule: no human has clicked it.

#### The case-folding backstop asks the volume, not the case table

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-projects/src/mount.rs `refusal_for`; reachable only through project_create / project_update / project_reconcile_skills, which no renderer issues`
- **Wrong** The rule is real and mutation-proven — forcing the `MountOccupant::Named(stored) if already_mounted.contains(...)` guard false reddened `what_the_volume_says_about_the_path_decides_whether_it_may_be_written` and `a_collision_the_case_table_misses_is_still_caught_by_the_filesystem`, one of which sources its colliding pair from a real NTFS 8.3 short-name alias — but a user cannot reach it, because the only doors are project commands nothing calls.
- **Done looks like** In the running window: enable two skills on one project whose directory names differ only by case, or whose 8.3 alias collides, and read one INVALID_PAYLOAD refusal naming both — not a silent dedupe and not two entries. Break it by neutering the already-mounted guard and watch the window report two mounted skills where the volume holds one directory entry.

Break it: make the first_collision branch unreachable and watch the window accept the colliding write and then show one mount where the user asked for two.

#### Working directory: read, never created, never deleted

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-projects/src/workdir.rs (8 tests, none mutated by the auditor — graded 'test unproven'); no renderer sets or displays a working directory`

Plus one mutation to close the 'test unproven' half: make the app-data-escape check return Ok and watch a named workdir test go red.

#### MCP stdio transport, end to end

- **Where** `src/data/mcp-repository.ts:109 (`adapter.invoke('mcp_list_tools', {})`) — UNREACHABLE from src/main.tsx, only importer is its own .test.ts; src-tauri/crates/vela-mcp/tests/stdio_end_to_end.rs (11 e2e tests against a real node child over real pipes)`
- **Wrong** Handshake, tools/list, tools/call, oversized-line refusal, stderr-is-not-failure, death mid-request and spawn failure are all covered against a real process — and nothing in the UI shows any of it. There is no MCP mention anywhere under src/features, src/app or src/components (re-derived at HEAD: `git grep -ln 'mcp\\|Mcp\\|MCP' -- src/features src/app src/components` returns nothing).
- **Done looks like** With a real stdio MCP server configured, open the running window and see its tools listed with names and descriptions; kill the child process from Task Manager and see the list report that server unavailable with a reason on the next read, while the other configured servers keep working.

#### MCP child process environment isolation

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-mcp/src/stdio.rs:146 `command.env_clear()`, with a 14-name INHERITED_ENV allowlist and its own no-credentials guard`
- **Wrong** Mutation-proven at the crate level — deleting env_clear() reddened `the_server_does_not_inherit_the_parent_environment` with 'an undeclared variable of the parent process reached the child' — but no MCP server is ever spawned by a user action, so the guarantee has never protected an actual user's environment.
- **Done looks like** Export a secret-looking variable into the user's environment, launch the app with a configured server whose tool echoes its own environment back, call it from the window, and confirm the variable is absent from the result and that exactly the 14 allowlisted names are present. Observed in the window, not in a test harness.

#### MCP config file: partial failure, disabled entries, missing file

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-mcp/src/config.rs (7 tests) and src-tauri/src/ipc/mcp.rs (6 more); McpHost::under_data_dir reads it once at src-tauri/src/lib.rs:107; no authoring or diagnostic surface anywhere under src/`
- **Wrong** The semantics are right — a missing file is an empty config, a non-JSON file is a whole-file failure carried on every list, one bad entry does not take the others down, read once at launch with no reload and none claimed. But there is no surface that shows the file's path, its parse failure, or which entries are disabled, so a user with a typo in mcp-servers.json gets silence, and the 'changes take effect next launch' rule is a code comment nobody can read.
- **Done looks like** Put a syntax error in mcp-servers.json, launch, and read a sentence in the window naming the file and the failure. Fix it, relaunch, and see the servers. Disable one entry and see it listed as disabled rather than simply missing. The 'edits take effect at next launch' sentence appears on that screen. Break it: make the whole-file failure return an empty config instead and watch the window go quiet where it used to name the file.

#### MCP pool: one process per server, restart on death, tool-list cache

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-mcp/src/pool.rs; tests the_pool_reuses_one_process_per_server (compares pids), the_pool_replaces_a_server_that_died, a_list_changed_notification_invalidates_the_cached_tool_list — all against the real fixture process`
- **Wrong** Three genuinely hard properties, all measured against a real child process, all reachable only through mcp_list_tools, which no shipping module calls. Nothing a user does causes a pool to be created outside a test.
- **Done looks like** In the running window with one server configured: refresh the tool list several times and confirm exactly one child process in Task Manager throughout. Kill it and confirm the next read brings a new pid and a fresh list without a relaunch. Have the server emit notifications/tools/list_changed and watch the list in the window change on its own.


### Conversation surface

#### Tool-call surface on the ordinary chat path  **[BLOCKING]**

- **Where** `src/data/chat-repository.ts:26-37 (StreamTurnRequest has no tools/toolChoice field at all) and :95-100 (the chat_send invoke passes exactly turnId, providerId, modelId, messages); src/platform/contract.ts:761-762 (ChatSendReq.tools / .toolChoice); src/features/conversation/use-conversation.ts:747 (the agent path is the only caller that offers tools)`
- **Wrong** The plain chat path can never offer a tool: the repository's request interface has no field to carry one, so it is not merely an omitted argument. The host is complete on the other side — ChatSendReq accepts both with #[serde(default)], build_request validates both, and eleven Rust tests cover that validation. Consequence recorded by the auditor: ToolCatalogueWithheld (emulation.rs:78, fires only when !request.tools.is_empty()) and ToolCallingEmulated cannot occur on the plain path at all, so their wording in notices.ts is unit-tested and unreachable from an ordinary chat.
- **Done looks like** With the agent toggle OFF, open a conversation on a tool-calling model, pick a tool from a renderer surface, send a prompt that needs it, and watch a tool-call card appear in the transcript — then repeat on a model whose capability probe reports no native tool calling and watch the 'Tools were described in the prompt' notice appear. Break it by dropping `tools` from the chat_send payload and watch a test that asserts the outgoing ChatSendReq carries the catalogue go red.

#### Tool-call surface on the ordinary chat path

- **Where** `src/data/mcp-repository.ts:108 (createMcpRepository); re-derived: the only callers of createMcpRepository/toolCatalogue/unavailableServers anywhere in src/ are src/data/mcp-repository.test.ts`
- **Wrong** The MCP tool catalogue is host-complete and on no import graph. `mcp_list_tools` is a registered command (contract.ts:1568), the repository projects server tools into exactly the ToolDefinitionInput[] shape ChatSendReq.tools and RunRequest.tools take, unavailable servers are separated out with reasons for a UI that does not exist — and no file outside its own test imports any of it. So even after the previous item is wired, there is nothing to offer.
- **Done looks like** From the running window: configure an MCP server, open a conversation, and see its tools listed as available (and a misconfigured server listed as unavailable with its reason). Send a prompt that calls one and see the call and its result in the transcript. Acceptance for the wiring alone: deleting createMcpRepository must break a production import, not just a test.

#### Tool-call surface on the ordinary chat path

- **Where** `src/features/conversation/MessageTurn.tsx:74 (<ToolCalls outcomes={turn.outcomes} progress={turn.toolProgress} /> — no results, no running); src/features/conversation/TurnNotices.tsx:42-63; src/features/conversation/tool-calls.ts:103-113 and :306 (collectToolResults); src/features/conversation/index.ts:24 (exported, no production caller); src/features/conversation/use-conversation.ts:773-798 (the run subscription handles only 'chat' and 'runFinished')`
- **Wrong** ToolCallList's 'result came back' and 'currently executing' states, and their twenty unit tests, are reachable only from tests. Neither `results` nor `running` has a production caller, and `collectToolResults` — which would build the results view out of the transcript's own toolResult parts — is exported and called by nothing but its test. This is not fixed by the agent path: agent-loop-harness.ts:430 really does execute tool calls, but use-conversation's subscription drops every run event that is not `chat` or `runFinished`, so no result ever reaches the view.
- **Done looks like** Turn the agent toggle on, send a prompt that makes the model spawn a subagent, and watch the tool card move through 'running' (spinner, live) and then show the returned content in the running window — and, after a restart, see the same result rebuilt from the stored toolResult parts via collectToolResults. Break it: drop the `running` prop and watch a test that asserts a live card is drawn as running go red.

#### Degradation notices: wording, totality, and reachability

- **Where** `src/features/conversation/notices.ts:62-73 (structuredOutputUnsupported / structuredOutputMismatch arms); src/platform/contract.ts:749-763 (ChatSendReq has no schema field); src/runtime/agent-loop-harness.ts:361-362 (forwards only tools and toolChoice)`
- **Wrong** Two of describeDegradation's eleven arms cannot fire in the shipped app on either path. Re-derived at HEAD: ChatSendReq carries turnId/providerId/modelId/messages/tools/toolChoice and nothing else, so no renderer can request a response schema; the agent loop forwards only tools and toolChoice; and the auditor confirmed build_request never calls with_schema. The Rust side implements schema handling, mismatch detection and a bounded MAX_MISMATCH_DETAIL_CHARS sanitiser for a request no surface can make. The row was graded PASS on totality and wording, but its reachability half is tests-only.
- **Done looks like** Either (a) a renderer surface requests structured output on a turn, and pointing it at a model that ignores schemas shows 'Structured output is not supported here' in the running window while a model that returns off-schema JSON shows 'The reply did not match the requested shape' with Vela's own validator wording — or (b) the two arms and the Rust path behind them are deleted and the union shrinks, so a fresh critic finds no notice in notices.ts that nothing can produce.

#### Ordinary-path write-on-settle (an interrupted turn is never recorded)

- **Where** `src/features/conversation/use-conversation.ts:978-996 (both messages appended only once the turn settles); src/data/transcript-repository.ts:64 (update → store_update_message); src-tauri/src/lib.rs:239 (registered); src/runtime/agent-loop-harness.ts:375-391 (the agent path already opens a row and updates its status)`
- **Wrong** The plain chat path writes the question and the answer only when the turn settles, so an ordinary turn interrupted by a window close is lost entirely — the question the user typed included. The mechanism for doing better is host-complete and already in production use on the other path: store_update_message is registered, transcript-repository exposes update(), and the agent-loop harness opens a streaming row and updates it to cancelled/failed. The plain path calls append only. The file's header acknowledges the gap and defers it; no renderer closes it.
- **Done looks like** Start a long reply in the running window, kill the app mid-stream, reopen the conversation: the question must be there, and the partial answer must be there marked as interrupted rather than as a complete reply (or absent with a stated reason) — never a conversation that lost the message the user typed. Break it by removing the update call and watch a named test that kills the turn mid-stream and re-lists the transcript go red.


### Agentic runtime and tool execution

#### A conversation view re-attaches to a run in flight on mount (LiveRuns.forConversation)  **[BLOCKING]**

- **Where** `src/runtime/live-runs.ts:312 (forConversation, implemented); src/features/conversation/use-conversation.ts:280-293 (unmount cleanup), :768 (the only subscribe in the product), :849-861 (stop reads activeRun.current); src/app/App.tsx:165 (key={conversationId ?? 'none'})`
- **Wrong** The directory can answer 'is a run live in this conversation' and no mount ever asks. `git grep forConversation -- src/` at HEAD returns only contract-harness.ts:1496 (declaration), live-runs.ts:312 (implementation) and three test files — zero callers under src/features, src/app, src/components, src/state. App.tsx:165 remounts ConversationSurface on the conversation id; the unmount at use-conversation.ts:287-292 unsubscribes and explicitly does NOT cancel; the new mount's activeRun ref is null, so stop() at :853 returns without reaching the orphan and `streaming` is false so the composer draws Send rather than Stop. Sending again is refused RUN_BUSY ('Another run is already going in this conversation.', use-conversation.ts:107-110) until DEFAULT_RUN_LIMITS.wallClockMs (contract-harness.ts:1018 = 10 * 60 * 1000) expires. agent-run.test.tsx:205 asserts this failure mode as if it were the design.
- **Done looks like** In a packaged Tauri build against a tool-calling model: turn the agent toggle on, send a prompt that streams for more than 30 seconds, click a different conversation mid-stream, click back. The transcript must show the run still streaming with the tokens produced while you were away, the composer must show Stop, and clicking Stop must end the run immediately and close the row as cancelled. Then break it: delete the forConversation call added on mount and watch a named test fail — and note that agent-run.test.tsx:205 ('says the conversation is busy when a run outlived the mount that started it') must be rewritten to assert re-attachment, because as written it locks in the bug.

#### RunSnapshot / LiveRuns.list — the directory's read side

- **Where** `src/runtime/live-runs.ts (list, RunHandle.snapshot); src/platform/contract-harness.ts:1497-1498; no caller anywhere under src/features, src/app, src/components, src/state`
- **Wrong** `runs.list()` and `RunHandle.snapshot()` have no non-test caller at HEAD — every hit from `git grep 'runs\.list\\|\.snapshot()' -- src/` is in live-runs.test.ts or subagent-toolkit.test.ts. Everything they expose (status.step, retainedFrom, startedAtMs, degradations, replayedFrom) is computed for nobody. There is no surface anywhere in the product that can tell a user a run exists, how far into its 12 steps it is, or that a second conversation has one going.
- **Done looks like** A surface that reads list() — a run indicator in the status bar or a badge on sidebar rows. In a packaged build: start an agent run, watch the indicator count 'step 2 of 12', 'step 3 of 12' as turns advance; switch to another conversation and see the first one still badged as running. Then delete the list() call and watch the indicator go blank and a named test fail. A unit test against a run double does not close this — the click is the evidence.

#### Degradations reach the user (stepLimitReached, toolCallLimitReached, wallClockLimitReached, contextUnavailable)

- **Where** `src/features/conversation/use-conversation.ts:770-798 (listener handles only `chat` and `runFinished`), :499-513 (settleRun reads outcome.type and stopReason and nothing else); src/features/conversation/TurnNotices.tsx:17 (DegradationNotes, exists, takes the provider-level `Degradation` type); src/features/conversation/notices.ts (describeDegradation has arms for eleven provider kinds and none of the five RunDegradation kinds); src/platform/contract-harness.ts:1152-1164 (RunDegradation); src/runtime/agent-loop-harness.ts:311`
- **Wrong** The subscribe listener has exactly two arms — `if (event.type === 'chat')` and `if (event.type !== 'runFinished') return;` — so `degraded`, `turnStarted`, `contextLoaded`, `toolCallStarted` and `toolCallFinished` are dropped on the floor. A run that hit its 12-step ceiling, spent its 32-call budget, ran out of its 10-minute clock, or ran without material the caller named renders identically to one that finished because the model stopped talking. agent-loop-harness.ts:311 cites conventions §9 ('a run that quietly ran with less than it was asked to is the silent reduction conventions §9 forbids') as the reason the event is emitted; the event is emitted and the reduction is still silent. The renderer half already exists (DegradationNotes with its own styling and aria-label) — it is the run-level type and the copy in notices.ts that are missing.
- **Done looks like** In a dev build with DEFAULT_RUN_LIMITS.maxSteps temporarily set to 1, run an agent turn that needs two steps and read a note under the answer, in the existing DegradationNotes list, saying the run stopped at its step ceiling. Repeat for the wall clock by setting wallClockMs to 5000. Then delete the `degraded` arm from the listener and watch a named test in agent-run.test.tsx fail. Acceptance requires all five RunDegradation kinds to have wording in notices.ts, or an explicit exhaustiveness failure at compile time if one does not.

Restart and confirm the setting survived. Break it by having the renderer send permission='ask' on a submit and watch the host ignore it because the level is host-held.


### Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest

#### `SettingsRepository.hasCredential` / `secrets_status` is tests-only (graded inside the PASS row 'Renderer credential surface is write-only and reaches a real UI')

- **Where** `src/data/settings-repository.ts:46-47 (interface), :77-80 (`adapter.invoke('secrets_status', …)`); callers at HEAD are only src/data/settings-repository.test.ts:102, :116, :184; adapter side src/platform/browser-adapter.ts:860; contract src/platform/contract.ts:1605, :1671`
- **Wrong** The auditor graded the credential surface PASS/reaches-user by tracing ModelWorkspace.tsx -> use-providers.ts -> settings-repository.ts -> `secrets_set`. That trace covers two of the three secrets commands. Re-derived at HEAD: `git grep hasCredential -- src/` finds the interface declaration, the implementation, and three test call sites — no product call site anywhere. `secrets_status` is in COMMAND_ALLOWLIST, in contract.ts, in generate_handler!, handled by BrowserAdapter, exercised by browser-adapter.test.ts and security-posture-parity.test.ts, and invoked by no shipping renderer code. The UI reads `credentialPresent` off the settings snapshot instead (EndpointsPanel.tsx:179). A tests-only method on a shipped interface is exactly the parallel-list defect this repo keeps finding.
- **Done looks like** Either (a) a product surface calls it and you can see the effect in the window — after typing a key and pressing save, the Endpoints row flips to 'key stored' without a full settings reload — and deleting that call site makes a named test fail; or (b) `hasCredential` is removed from SettingsRepository and `secrets_status` is either removed from contract.ts + COMMAND_ALLOWLIST + generate_handler! (with `rust_and_typescript_allowlists_are_identical` and handler_binding.rs still green) or documented in contract.ts as host-internal with a stated reason it has no renderer caller. What must not survive is an unexplained command whose only callers are tests.

#### `gate_m_debug_log_acl.rs` — the only out-of-process DACL cross-check — runs in no job

- **Where** `src-tauri/tests/gate_m_debug_log_acl.rs (`#[ignore]`d, gated on VELA_GATE_DEBUG_LOG_DATA_DIR — see its own docs at :30); .github/workflows/ci.yml:186 `test-windows` job runs plain `cargo test --workspace --locked``
- **Wrong** Every guard that runs in CI reads the DACL back through `private_fs::describe` — the same module that wrote it. The one driver that verifies out of process with `Get-Acl` is #[ignore]d and needs an environment variable, so `cargo test --workspace` on windows-latest skips it. A bug in `private_fs`'s own read-back (as opposed to its write) would be invisible to every automated guard in the repo. The auditor recorded this as by design; it means the cross-check is a manual gate, not a regression guard, and the report row 'Something holds the DACL going forward' should carry that asterisk.
- **Done looks like** A step in the test-windows job that sets VELA_GATE_DEBUG_LOG_DATA_DIR to a scratch directory and runs `cargo test -p vela-app --test gate_m_debug_log_acl -- --ignored`, green on a push. Break it by making the Win32 `apply()` a no-op and watch THAT CI step fail — distinct from the four in-process private_fs tests, which already fail under the same mutation.

The user sees only the collapsed boolean flags.

#### Structured-output validation (MEASURED-5) — no user path can request it

- **Where** `src-tauri/src/ipc/chat.rs:234 (build_request); src/platform/contract.ts:749-763 (ChatSendReq); src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/src/structured.rs (468 lines); src/features/conversation/notices.ts:62-73`
- **Wrong** Re-ran at HEAD: `git grep ResponseFormat\|response_format\|responseFormat -- src-tauri/src src` is empty. ChatSendReq carries turnId, providerId, modelId, messages, tools, toolChoice and nothing else, so build_request leaves ChatRequest.response_format at its default (model.rs:296). structured.rs — MachineText, StructuredOutputPolicy, schema validation, the type that cannot be built from a &str — is exercised only by the capability probe, which sends its own json_schema request. The two notices written for the feature, 'Structured output is not supported here' and 'The reply did not match the requested shape' (notices.ts:62-73), can never fire.

On the llama.cpp model on :8033 — which the live probe recorded as answering 200 while ignoring the schema — get 'The reply did not match the requested shape' under the turn instead of silently wrong prose. Also confirm the 'Structured output: Not offered' row prevents the request being offered at all on a model probed as not honouring it.

#### Reasoning-request controls, sampling, cache hints and max_context_tokens are never set by any caller

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/src/model.rs:296-303 (ChatRequest); src-tauri/src/ipc/chat.rs:234 (build_request); src/platform/contract.ts:749-763`
- **Wrong** ChatRequest carries response_format, reasoning (ReasoningRequest), cache (CacheHints), sampling (Sampling), max_output_tokens and max_context_tokens. `git grep ReasoningRequest\|with_reasoning` and `git grep Sampling\|CacheHints\|max_context_tokens\|maxContextTokens` over src-tauri/src and src are both empty at HEAD. The IPC request type has no field for any of them and build_request sets none, so all five ride at their defaults forever. In particular ContextBudget::for_request's max_context_tokens fallback is dead code: context fitting is driven entirely by discovery, with no way for a user or the host to override a window the endpoint reports wrongly.
- **Done looks like** From the model bar, raise and lower thinking effort on a reasoning model and watch two otherwise identical turns differ in visible thinking and reasoning-token count. Set a temperature and see two runs of the same prompt diverge. Set a context ceiling below the endpoint's declared window and watch the 'The conversation was shortened' notice fire earlier than it otherwise would. None of these controls exists today.

### Conversation surface

#### Retry button targeting on an older failed turn

- **Where** `src/features/conversation/use-conversation.ts:863-870 (retry); src/features/conversation/ConversationView.tsx:137 (onRetry={conversation.retry} handed to every AssistantTurn); src/features/conversation/MessageTurn.tsx:86 and :112 (the two 'Try again' buttons)`
- **Wrong** The button is per-turn; retry() is global. It does `[...current].reverse().find(kind === 'user')` and replaces everything from the LAST user message, so clicking 'Try again' under an older failed turn re-sends the newest question, discards the newest answer, and (in a persisted conversation) transcript.remove()s that answer's store rows — while the failed turn the user was trying to fix is left exactly as it was. The auditor's probe recorded SENT_AFTER_RETRY re-sending QUESTION TWO from QUESTION ONE's only button. Nothing blocks the composer after a failure (blockedReason covers only 'no model chosen' and 'transcript unreadable'), so this is an ordinary sequence, not a corner.
- **Done looks like** In the running window: send Q1 and make it fail (point the model at a dead port), send Q2 and let it answer, then click 'Try again' inside Q1's error box. Q1 must re-run in place — its error box is replaced by a new streaming reply — and Q2's question AND its answer must still be on screen, and still there after closing and reopening the app. Then break it: change retry to take an entry id and point it back at the last user entry, and watch a named test that drives the two-turn transcript and clicks the older button go red.

#### What a user sees when a model returns nothing

- **Where** `src/features/conversation/MessageTurn.tsx:38-131 (AssistantTurn has no branch for a settled turn with no answer, no reasoning, no error, no refusal, no outcomes); reachable per src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/src/stream.rs:335, anthropic/stream.rs:619, google/stream.rs:559`
- **Wrong** A clean zero-token stream (immediate EOS, empty completion, a filter that stopped generation without setting an error) settles as `done` with empty parts. Every branch in AssistantTurn is skipped: no spinner (phase !== 'awaiting'), no Markdown (answer === ''), no 'reasoning but no answer text' (reasoning === ''), no notices, no copy button, no usage, no 'Stopped'. The probe measured EMPTY_COMPLETE_TURN => "" — a literally empty <article aria-label="Model reply">. The three Rust adapters raise StreamEndedWithoutAnswer only when malformed_frames > 0, so a clean empty stream is not classified as an error anywhere and the user gets a blank turn with nothing to act on. The reasoning-only case has a sentence for exactly this situation; the no-output case does not.
- **Done looks like** Point the composer at an endpoint (or a fixture host) that returns a well-formed completion with zero content and press Send in the running window: the turn must carry a Vela-authored sentence saying the model returned no text, and a 'Try again' button. Then break it: delete that branch and watch a named test assert that a settled AssistantTurn's textContent is never empty go red. A fresh critic should be able to render every TurnState the reducer can produce and find none whose article renders to "".

- **Wrong** stopReason 'maxTokens' survives the stream, the state and the store round-trip and is rendered nowhere. ### Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus

Adding a block containing `pnpm brand-new-gate` to test-windows left `every gate command in the workflow is accounted for` GREEN, while a single-line `- run: pnpm brand-new-gate` failed it. ci.yml already writes its apt steps in block form at lines 60 and 122, which is why `!line.startsWith('sudo apt-get')` at line 96 is unreachable dead code — proof the hole is not hypothetical.

Then revert and confirm the real apt blocks at ci.yml:60/122 produce no false failure — i.e. line 96's exemption is now actually reached, which a critic can check by deleting it and watching the test go red.

#### `pnpm verify` is runnable on Windows, the platform Vela ships on (FAIL, shipped: not-wired)  **[BLOCKING]**

- **Where** `package.json:18-19 (`test:secrets`, `test:transcripts` invoke `./scripts/*.sh`), package.json:25 (the nine-step verify chain); .npmrc (no `script-shell`); .github/workflows/ci.yml:236 sets `shell: bash` for the same script`
- **Wrong** pnpm runs lifecycle scripts through cmd.exe on Windows and `.npmrc` sets no `script-shell`, so `pnpm run test:transcripts` and `pnpm run test:secrets` both die with "'.' is not recognized as an internal or external command". The chain is typecheck && lint:rust && test && test:harness && build && test:transcripts && test:secrets && cargo build --workspace --locked && cargo test --workspace --locked, so it stops at step 6 of 9 and NEITHER cargo gate ever runs on the machine that builds the product. The CI author knew bash was required — ci.yml sets `shell: bash` explicitly for check-transcripts.sh — package.json carries no equivalent.
- **Done looks like** On a Windows box, `pnpm verify` runs past step 6: secret-scan prints its `12 passed, 0 failed`, check-transcripts prints its verdict, and the chain reaches and completes `cargo build --workspace --locked` and `cargo test --workspace --locked`. Then plant an AWS-shaped key in a tracked file and watch `pnpm verify` go RED at test:secrets — not die at "'.' is not recognized". A critic must see verify fail for the reason the gate exists, not for the shell.

- **Wrong** `const contractKeys: (keyof IpcContract)[] = [...]` is a hand-written list. That type constrains every element to BE a key; it does not require the list to COVER the keys. Adding `zz_audit_probe: { req: EmptyPayload; res: Ack }` to `IpcContract` and to nothing else passed all 24 platform test files / 1187 tests, including contract.test.ts and the 80-case adapter-parity. The invariant does hold, but only incidentally: `browser-adapter.ts`'s exhaustive switch bottoms out in `never`, so `pnpm exec tsc --build --force` reports `browser-adapter.ts(909,15): TS2322 Type '"zz_audit_probe"' is not assignable to type 'never'`. A refactor of that switch silently kills the guarantee, and two comments point maintainers at a runtime test that does nothing of the kind.
- **Done looks like** Add `zz_audit_probe: { req: EmptyPayload; res: Ack }` to `IpcContract` in contract.ts and to nothing else, then run `pnpm exec vitest run src/platform/contract.test.ts` and watch it FAIL naming `zz_audit_probe`. Second, harder acceptance: delete or collapse the exhaustive switch in browser-adapter.ts and confirm the vitest guard STILL fires — proving the guard no longer borrows its teeth from an unrelated file. Both comments must then describe the mechanism that actually ran.

### Sandbox and code execution

#### `processes` limit reported `kernel`-enforced and not enforced at all (FAIL, hardware)  **[BLOCKING]**

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-sandbox/src/wsl.rs:177-178 (comment `RLIMIT_NPROC, set before privileges are dropped` + `processes: EnforcementLevel::Kernel`); wsl.rs:322-325 (`ulimit -u {} 2>/dev/null \|\| true`); wsl.rs:403 (`printf %s {script} \| base64 -d \| /bin/sh`); src-tauri/crates/vela-sandbox/tests/sandbox_boundary.rs:1582,1606`
- **Wrong** `processes` is the only limit this backend claims at kernel strength. It is set with `ulimit -u`, in a script piped to `/bin/sh`, which on Ubuntu is dash — dash's ulimit has no `-u` (`/bin/sh: 3: ulimit: Illegal option -u`), and `2>/dev/null \|\| true` swallows the error so `set -e` never sees it. Measured through the real SandboxHost: the grant handed to the caller said `processes: 8` while the run's actual RLIMIT_NPROC was 127929 and it forked 300 processes with zero errors. The only test that mentions `processes` (sandbox_boundary.rs:1582-1606) asserts the grant NUMBER against `unreachable_backend()` — it never runs a program, so it asserts exactly the field the defect makes untrue. Because /bin/sh is dash on some distros and bash on others, and WslBackend::detect() takes the first non-Docker distribution whatever it is, a hardcoded EnforcementLevel here is a claim that varies by machine.
- **Done looks like** A new integration test in sandbox_boundary.rs that submits through the real WslBackend with `limits.processes = N`, and from inside the guest asserts that `/proc/self/limits` reads N for max processes AND that the N+1th fork fails. That test must be red at HEAD before any fix. Then either enforce it with something dash executes (prlimit --nproc, or set it in the setpriv step before privileges drop) and confirm deleting that one line turns the new test red again; or change wsl.rs:178 to `EnforcementLevel::Unenforced` with the same kind of explanatory paragraph memory_bytes and cpu_millicores already carry, and delete the RLIMIT_NPROC sentence at wsl.rs:177. Either way, a fresh critic must be able to run one program inside the guest, print /proc/self/limits, and find it agrees with what report() says.

#### No test takes the shipped binary's only reachable path: Ask → awaitingApproval → valid-digest approval → the run actually starting (derived during this pass; not a row in the audit table)  **[BLOCKING]**

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-sandbox/tests/sandbox_boundary.rs — `PermissionLevel::Full` appears at 20 sites, every real-execution escape test among them; `ApprovalDecision::AllowOnce` appears exactly once, at line 594, and it is a deliberately WRONG digest that must be rejected. The resume path itself is host.rs:293-315 (approve → notify_all) and host.rs:478-490 (the wait loop the run thread parks in)`
- **Wrong** src-tauri/src/ipc/sandbox.rs:97 hardcodes `PermissionLevel::Ask`, so `Ask` is the only level the shipped app can ever be in. Every test that executes a real program inside the WSL boundary constructs the host at `PermissionLevel::Full`, which the shipped app can never reach. The two `Ask` tests that use a real backend both end in denial (line 602) or abandonment. The only `AllowOnce` in the whole battery is the negative case at line 594. So the resume-after-approval code — the run thread parked in host.rs's wait loop, woken by approve(), then launching — is executed by no test, and it is the only path a shipped run can take. Every confinement guarantee the escape battery proves is proved on a code path the binary cannot enter.
- **Done looks like** An integration test at `PermissionLevel::Ask` with a real backend that waits for AwaitingApproval, approves with the digest the host actually handed out, and then asserts the same things the Full-level escape tests assert — the program ran, its stdout came back, /mnt is unreachable, the rootfs is read-only. Breaking `approve`'s notify_all (host.rs:309) must make it hang and fail rather than pass. Until that test exists, no escape claim in this crate has been demonstrated on the path the app uses.

#### stderr fidelity — host bytes attributed to the program (FAIL, hardware)

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-sandbox/src/host.rs:580-640 (stdout is drained byte-by-byte to READY_SENTINEL at 588-604 before the pumps start; `spawn_pump(stderr, OutputStream::Stderr, ...)` at line 640 then reads everything the pipe buffered from BEFORE confinement); src-tauri/crates/vela-sandbox/src/wsl.rs:452-461 (new_command's env_clear, which fixes only the environment-caused class); assertion at tests/sandbox_boundary.rs:391`
- **Wrong** stdout has a ready-sentinel gate and stderr has none. On a cold VM a run delivered wsl.exe's own UTF-16LE warning ("the wsl2.localhostForwarding setting has no effect when using mirrored networking mode" — triggered by the user's own .wslconfig, not by anything Vela did) as SandboxEvent::Output{stream: Stderr}, counted against output_bytes, before a byte of the program had run. new_command's comment anticipates exactly this class for untranslatable PATH entries and fixes it with env_clear; this warning is not environmental and gets through. It also makes `output_arrives_in_the_programs_own_order_within_one_stream` order-dependent: sandbox_boundary.rs:391 asserts stderr equals "to-stderr\n" EXACTLY, and it only passes because an earlier test in the same run warmed the VM.
- **Done looks like** A test whose backend emits a line on the launcher's stderr before the ready sentinel arrives on stdout, asserting three things: no Output{stream:Stderr} event carries that line, RunUsage.output_bytes does not count its bytes, and the run still settles normally. Then, separately, `wsl --terminate Ubuntu` and run `cargo test -p vela-sandbox --test sandbox_boundary output_arrives_in_the_programs_own_order_within_one_stream -- --test-threads=1` as the only test in a fresh process against the cold distribution — it must pass. It fails today or passes only by luck of ordering.

#### Test coverage for the descendant-reaping claim (FAIL, test unproven)

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-sandbox/tests/sandbox_boundary.rs:434-475 — the whole descendant clause rests on `!collector.text(Stdout).contains("NEVER")` at lines 472-473; the mechanism it is named for is `--kill-child` at src-tauri/crates/vela-sandbox/src/wsl.rs:396`
- **Wrong** The auditor removed `--kill-child` from the launcher and `cancelling_stops_the_run_and_reaches_every_descendant` still passed. Its only descendant-facing assertion reads a pipe the host stops reading the moment the launcher dies, so an orphaned guest process could print NEVER into a closed pipe forever and the assertion would still hold. The behaviour was separately measured to be correct on this machine (2 markers alive during, 0 after, with and without the flag) — so this is a hole in the evidence, not currently a hole in the boundary. But the test's name is exactly what a future reviewer would cite as cover before deleting the flag.
- **Done looks like** The test spawns a `setsid`-detached grandchild that appends a timestamped marker every 200ms to a file on the host side of a readWrite bind mount, cancels the run, waits five marker intervals, and asserts the file's last mtime predates the cancel — a channel that survives the pipe closing. Removing `--kill-child` from wsl.rs:396 must turn that test red, and putting it back must turn it green. Until that mutation bites, the test name is a claim nothing holds.

### Scheduler, dual local endpoint, memory, canvas/artifacts

#### Cadence semantics and catch-up counting

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/model.rs:800-803 (documented limitation), :825 (Cadence::advance), interval_ms`
- **Wrong** Daily is exactly 86,400,000 ms and Weekly a fixed multiple of it, not 'the same wall-clock time'. There is no timezone column on a schedule. A daily schedule drifts one hour across a DST boundary and drifts permanently after a clock change. The consequence is written down at model.rs:800-803 and is guarded by nothing — the auditor found no test in the repo touching DST, timezones or a clock jump.
- **Done looks like** A test that stands a daily schedule at 09:00 local, advances the clock across a DST transition, and asserts the next fire is still 09:00 local — and that test goes red when Cadence::advance is reverted to fixed millisecond offsets. In the window: a schedule created for 9am still fires at 9am the week after the clocks change.

#### Canvas sandbox host is host-held, and artifact execution can be switched off

- **Where** `src/platform/contract-sandbox.ts:1219 (DEFAULT_AUTO_APPROVAL_PROFILE document floor 'ownRendererProcess'); src/features/canvas/document-run.ts:52-55 (CANVAS_ISOLATION_FLOOR 'opaqueOriginFrame'), :130-135 (autoApproves)`
- **Wrong** autoApproves builds the floor from the profile's document level ('ownRendererProcess') and requires isolationMeets(request.minimumIsolation, floor); every Canvas submit asks for CANVAS_ISOLATION_FLOOR ('opaqueOriginFrame'), one rank below. The comparison can therefore never be true for a Canvas request, so a user who sets permission to 'approve' is prompted exactly as if they had set 'ask'. document-run.ts:122-128 says this is intended until stronger isolation exists — which means the whole auto-approval branch is dead code that a reader will assume works.
- **Done looks like** Either: with permission set to 'approve' in the running window, a model draws three SVGs in a row and none of them prompts — and lowering the request below the profile floor does prompt. Or: the branch is deleted and the profile's document floor is removed, so nothing in the tree implies an automatic answer that cannot happen. A reviewer must be able to tell which of the two was chosen without reading a comment.

#### Canvas refuses languages it cannot draw and still shows source

- **Where** `src/features/canvas/document-frame.ts:142-143 (case 'html' falls through to case 'react'), :152-153 (case 'svg' falls through to case 'mermaid'); src/features/canvas/document-host.ts:176 (CANVAS_LANGUAGES = ['html','svg']), :418`
- **Wrong** frameFor still has live 'react' and 'mermaid' arms that inline raw JSX into an HTML body and raw Mermaid text into an SVG-styled document. Neither is reachable today because document-host.ts:418 refuses the submit languageUnsupported first, but the arms are a loaded gun: the day CANVAS_LANGUAGES grows to include either, the frame renders garbage rather than failing, and no test covers the rendered output of those two arms.
- **Done looks like** Add 'react' to CANVAS_LANGUAGES in a scratch branch and watch a test fail loudly rather than a broken frame draw. Acceptance for the fix: either the two arms are deleted so the switch is exhaustive over CANVAS_LANGUAGES only, or each grows a real renderer with a test asserting the drawn document, and adding the language to CANVAS_LANGUAGES makes an artifact actually render in the window.


### Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest

#### The rest of the application-data directory is NOT owner-only (FAIL, hardware)  **[BLOCKING]**

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/location.rs:65 (bare `std::fs::create_dir_all(parent)` in `DatabaseLocation::prepare`); src-tauri/src/store_host.rs:44-57 (`database_location` / `open` never harden); src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/src/private_fs.rs:188 `create_private_dir` — its ONLY caller at HEAD is src-tauri/src/ipc/diagnostics.rs:185; src-tauri/crates/vela-store/Cargo.toml declares no vela-providers dependency, so private_fs is not even reachable from the store crate`
- **Wrong** private_fs.rs was written because 'the application-data directory is already per-user' was measured false on Windows. The fix was applied to `diagnostics/` and to nothing else. Measured live on this machine: %APPDATA%\dev.vela.desktop\vela.db (315 KB, live), vela.db-wal (2.6 MB) and skills/ all report AreAccessRulesProtected=False with DESKTOP-298M5DU\CodexSandboxUsers ReadAndExecute inherited=True plus an app-container SID FullControl — the identical ACE private_fs.rs exists to remove. `private_fs::describe(vela.db).is_private()` would return false by the project's own definition. The database holds every prompt and answer verbatim, permanently and always-on; the debug log it was built to protect is opt-in and off by default. It is strictly the larger exposure of the same data class.
- **Done looks like** On Windows, widen the root first (`icacls %APPDATA%\dev.vela.desktop /grant *S-1-5-32-545:(OI)(CI)(RX)`), then launch the app, send one message, and quit. `Get-Acl` on dev.vela.desktop, vela.db, vela.db-wal, vela.db-shm and skills/ must every one report AreAccessRulesProtected: True with exactly owner + NT AUTHORITY\SYSTEM, inherited=False — the same reading diagnostics/ gives today. Then break it: make the Win32 `apply()` in private_fs.rs a no-op and watch a NAMED test that covers the database path go red on windows-latest, the way the four private_fs tests already do. If only the four private_fs tests go red, the store path is still unguarded.

#### Debug log lazy-open failure vs. reported status (UNVERIFIED, read only)

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/src/debuglog.rs:171 `crate::private_fs::open_private_append(path).ok()`; debuglog.rs:176-185 `FileSink::record` lazy open; src-tauri/src/ipc/diagnostics.rs `status_of` / `debug_log_get``
- **Wrong** `debug_log_set_with` fails closed correctly on the DIRECTORY — it refuses to install a sink, calls `debuglog::disable()`, and returns an IpcError naming the directory and the OS error. But the FILE is opened lazily on first record, through `.ok()`. If file hardening fails at that later moment, the Option is None, `FileSink::record` silently writes nothing, and `diagnostics_debug_log_get` keeps returning `enabled: true` with a path. It fails closed for secrecy and open for honesty: the user is told the log is recording and is pointed at a file that will never contain the `trace <id>` the error showed them. No test covers it and no auditor constructed one.
- **Done looks like** A test that hardens the directory successfully, then makes the file itself un-hardenable or un-openable (an injected enforcer that refuses on the file path, or an icacls-locked target), drives one `debuglog::record`, and asserts `diagnostics_debug_log_get` reports a state that is NOT `{enabled: true}` over an empty sink. In the running window: with the file un-openable, click the Debug Log switch on in the Endpoints panel and see the failure text render, not a green 'recording' plus a path. Break it by restoring `.ok()`-swallows-and-reports-enabled and watch that test go red.

#### `mcp-servers.json` at rest (could-not-establish; same root cause as the app-data DACL finding, different code path and different data class)

- **Where** `src-tauri/src/ipc/mcp.rs:41 `CONFIG_FILE_NAME = "mcp-servers.json"`; mcp.rs:60 `McpHost::under_data_dir(directory: PathBuf)`; src-tauri/src/lib.rs:101+ (read from `app.path().app_data_dir()`)`
- **Wrong** mcp-servers.json is read from the same unprotected application-data root, and an MCP server entry carries `env: BTreeMap<String, String>` — which is precisely where a user puts an API token, because that is what MCP configs are for. Unlike vela.db (whose schema provably has no credential column), this file can hold plaintext credential material by design. It gets no private_fs treatment at any point and would inherit the root's measured `AreAccessRulesProtected: False` with `CodexSandboxUsers ReadAndExecute`. The file is absent on this machine so no direct ACL reading exists — the exposure is inferred from the root's measured inheritance, which is why this is an entry and not a graded FAIL.
- **Done looks like** Write a mcp-servers.json containing an `env` entry into %APPDATA%\dev.vela.desktop, launch the app so McpHost reads it, quit, then `Get-Acl` the file: AreAccessRulesProtected: True, owner + SYSTEM only. Plus a test that deliberately widens that file before startup and asserts it comes back private, which goes red when the Win32 `apply()` is a no-op. If the decision is instead 'we do not harden it', mcp.rs must say so out loud next to the `env` field and the docs must stop implying the app-data directory is private.

#### `HttpRequest.headers` is a public plain-String escape from the SecretValue discipline

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/src/http.rs:130 `self.headers.push((name.clone(), value.expose().to_owned()))`; the field `pub headers: Vec<(String, String)>` on `HttpRequest`; read back at http.rs:1042-1044`
- **Wrong** This is one of five production `SecretValue::expose()` sites and the only one where the credential lands somewhere durable: it is copied into a plain `String` on a PUBLIC field. It is not zeroized, and SecretValue's zeroize-on-Drop cannot reach it (Clone already makes independent-drop copies, and this String is outside the wrapper entirely). Three things bound it — a hand-written redacting `Debug`, `redacted_headers()` for recorders, and no `Serialize` impl — and all three are conventions, not compiler enforcement. Any new code can write `format!("{}", v)` over a header value and no test would fail. The auditor searched and found no production reader that stringifies it; absence of a reader today is not a guard.
- **Done looks like** Either the field becomes private behind an accessor returning a redacted or secret-wrapped view, such that adding `format!("{}", request.headers[0].1)` outside the transport stops COMPILING; or a guard test scans the workspace for a read of `.headers` outside the transport module and fails — verified by adding such a read and watching it go red. Separately, any doc calling SecretValue's zeroize a memory-hygiene guarantee must be corrected to 'best-effort, and the header copy is outside it'.

### Storage, migrations and search

#### Two stores on one file / write-write concurrency

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/sqlite.rs:104-105 and :109 (`PRAGMA busy_timeout = 5000` and its rationale); the only coverage is `a_second_store_on_the_same_file_sees_the_first_ones_writes`, which races nothing; src-tauri/src/ipc/error.rs:100-105 maps every Backend error to one opaque string`

Click-equivalent: launch two Vela windows against the same %APPDATA% database, send a message in each within the same second, and confirm both transcripts contain both messages after a restart of each.

#### MCP HTTP/SSE transport

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-mcp/src/config.rs::resolve; src-tauri/crates/vela-mcp/src/lib.rs (declares the absence); src-tauri/src/ipc/mcp.rs test `a_remote_entry_is_listed_as_an_unsupported_transport``

If the transport is not going to be built this cycle, the acceptance test is the opposite: `git grep -in 'HTTP/SSE'` over docs and briefs returns nothing that claims it ships.

The missing piece is the command and the executor arm that would route to it.

### Conversation surface

#### Agent path: failure sentences vs failure rendering

- **Where** `src/features/conversation/agent-run.test.tsx (all 15 tests assert hook state, e.g. reply(result.current).turn.refusal); src/features/conversation/MessageTurn.tsx:77-91 (the refusal box the mutation deleted)`

The agent path's failure sentences are pinned; its failure rendering rides entirely on one plain-path test. Any future change that renders an agent failure differently — or not at all — is caught by nothing in agent-run.test.tsx.
- **Done looks like** At least one agent-path test renders ConversationSurface (not just the hook) and asserts the DOM for a refused run, a failed run and a cancelled run. Acceptance: delete MessageTurn.tsx:77-91 and an agent-run test must go red, not only the plain-path one.

#### Scroll: follow the stream only while pinned to the bottom

- **Where** `src/features/conversation/scroll.ts (pure, 10 tests); src/features/conversation/ConversationView.tsx (ResizeObserver, passive scroll listener, useLayoutEffect scrollTo — the file states this is unverifiable in jsdom)`
- **Wrong** Graded PASS on the pure geometry only. The plumbing that connects that geometry to a real viewport has no test that can run: jsdom has no layout, so the ResizeObserver wiring, the passive listener and the layout-effect scrollTo are inspection-only. The one defect this file's tests were written for (the 1280x672 display-scaling empty state) was a layout defect, which is exactly the class jsdom cannot see.
- **Done looks like** In the running window at 1280x672 with Windows display scaling on: start a long stream and watch the transcript follow the tail; scroll up 200px mid-stream and watch it stop following and not jump; scroll back to the bottom and watch it resume; resize the window mid-stream and confirm the pin survives. Recorded with a real-window check (CDP or equivalent) that a fresh critic can re-run, not a jsdom assertion.

No test in the suite exercises forced-colors.

### Build, packaging and developer experience

#### Bundle identifier `dev.vela.desktop` is shared by every instance

- **Where** `C:\Users\User\vela-tmp\src-tauri\tauri.conf.json:5; C:\Users\User\vela-tmp\src-tauri\crates\vela-secrets\src\lib.rs:47 (`KEYCHAIN_SERVICE`); C:\Users\User\vela-tmp\src-tauri\src\store_host.rs`
- **Wrong** `app.path().app_data_dir()` resolves to `%APPDATA%\dev.vela.desktop` for every instance, so two running Velas share one SQLite file, one projects tree, one skills store, one diagnostics jsonl, one keychain service name and one WebView2 `EBWebView` profile. `git grep -niE 'single.instance\|CreateMutex\|WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER' -- src src-tauri` returns zero hits, and Cargo.lock contains no `tauri-plugin-*` crate of any kind. docs/desktop-gate/VERDICTS.md:1414 records the hazard and leaves the mitigation as a manual instruction to whoever remembers — it cost that session twice.
- **Done looks like** Launch Vela, then launch it again while the first is running. Either the second refuses with a visible "Vela is already running" and focuses the first window, or each instance gets its own data root — confirmed by seeing two distinct `EBWebView` directories on disk and by creating a conversation in one instance and watching the other's list stay coherent rather than silently diverge. Then kill the first instance and confirm a fresh launch still starts (no stale lock).

#### An installable bundle has ever been produced

- **Where** `C:\Users\User\vela-tmp\src-tauri\tauri.conf.json:39-41 (`bundle.active: true`, `targets: "all"`); `src-tauri/target/release/bundle` does not exist; `src-tauri/target/release/wix/x64` exists and is empty (mtime 2026-08-13 17:10)`
- **Wrong** No installer has ever been produced. The empty `wix/x64` is where the Tauri CLI unpacks WiX 3.14, so a `pnpm tauri build` was started on 2026-08-13 and did not clear the bundler. There is no code-signing configuration and no updater configuration, so even a successful bundle yields an unsigned installer SmartScreen will warn on. WiX and NSIS are both downloaded from the internet at build time, in a product whose first line of description is "offline-first". conventions.md:468 honestly lists this as "not attempted".
- **Done looks like** `pnpm tauri build` produces an `.msi` and an `.exe` under `src-tauri/target/release/bundle`. Install the MSI on a machine that has never had Vela, launch it from the Start menu, watch the title bar and taskbar render the real icon (this is also the only end-to-end proof for the bundle-icon aspect), create a conversation, restart, and see it still there. Record the actual SmartScreen behaviour of the unsigned installer in conventions.md §11 instead of leaving the row "not attempted".

#### Build determinism / toolchain pinning

- **Where** `No `rust-toolchain.toml` anywhere (`git ls-files \| grep rust-toolchain` → nothing); C:\Users\User\vela-tmp\src-tauri\Cargo.toml:23 (`rust-version = "1.82"`); C:\Users\User\vela-tmp\.github\workflows\ci.yml:66, :138, :210 (`dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable`)`
- **Wrong** `rust-version` is a floor, not a pin, and `@stable` floats with the calendar. `Cargo.lock` plus `--locked` pin dependency versions but not the compiler, so two builds a month apart are two different compilers. The JS half is properly pinned — I confirmed `pnpm-lock.yaml` regenerates byte-identical after EOL normalisation. The bundler toolchains (WiX, NSIS) are additionally fetched over the network at build time.
- **Done looks like** A committed `rust-toolchain.toml` names an exact version, and the three `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` uses in ci.yml either pin the same version or defer to the file. Prove the file is load-bearing: bump it by one patch version, push, and watch the `rustc --version` line in a CI log change to match. Two builds of the same commit a month apart then report the same compiler.

The endpoint's 43 lib + 12 integration tests all run against a closure standing in for the model. If someone added a 'remember my debug log setting' key tomorrow, no test would fail — and DebugLogSwitch.tsx would then be telling the user something false in the product UI.
- **Done looks like** A test that inspects the sink slot in a fresh process with no `disable()` call and asserts it is None, PLUS a structural guard in the shape of the existing allowlist guards that scans settings keys and store columns for anything matching `debug.?log` and fails if one appears. Break it by adding a fake persisted key and watch the guard go red. In the running window: turn the log on, quit, relaunch, open the Endpoints panel, and see the switch unchecked before touching it.

#### `scripts/secret-scan.sh` covers four key shapes; the size of the gap is asserted, not measured

- **Where** `scripts/secret-scan.sh (patterns: `sk-ant-`, `sk-`, `AIza`, PEM); scripts/secret-scan.test.sh (12 cases, all green); .github/workflows/ci.yml:251 `secret-tripwire``
- **Wrong** The tripwire is genuinely good — it is its own mutation harness, it plants real key shapes in throwaway repos, `excluded_paths=()` is empty and a test fails the build if a glob appears in it, and the self-test runs BEFORE the scan so a broken scanner cannot report clean. But it matches four shapes. An Azure key, an AWS access-key pair, a bearer JWT, or the `--api-key` value a user invents for their own llama.cpp would be committed clean past a green CI. Nobody has planted one to confirm the miss, so the gap is documented rather than known.
- **Done looks like** Add a case to secret-scan.test.sh for each shape you decide to cover — at minimum an AWS `AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}` plus its 40-char secret, and a `eyJ...` JWT — watch each new case FAIL against the current scanner (that is the measurement), then add the pattern and watch it pass. Each new pattern also needs a negative case so prose containing the prefix cannot mute the tripwire, matching the existing 'prose that merely contains sk- is not a credential' case.


### Providers and model layer

#### Emulated tool calling has never been measured against a model that lacks native tool calling

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/src/emulation.rs; src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/tests/ (no target drives render_catalogue → parse_calls → render_results against a real endpoint); the only live endpoint available calls tools natively (`emulated: false` in the auditor's live turn)`
- **Wrong** The whole emulation round trip is fixture-and-mock evidence. The stripper half is guarded (it fell to the held_back mutation), but nobody has watched a real model that has no tools API be handed an inlined catalogue and have a call read back out of its prose. Blocked behind the gate defect above: even with such an endpoint configured, no surface would offer it a tool.
- **Done looks like** Serve a model with no tool-calling support (e.g. a base completion model on llama.cpp), configure it, and complete a tool call end to end in the window: the catalogue goes into the prompt, the model's textual call is parsed, the tool runs, the result is fed back, the final answer contains none of the call markup, and the emulated-tools notice is shown. Then hand it deliberately malformed call markup and confirm the call is shown to the user rather than executed.

#### Cancellation mid-turn and the stall timeout were never fired against a real socket

- **Where** `src-tauri/src/ipc/chat.rs (chat_cancel → CancelToken); src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/src/stream.rs::drive_stream (the tokio::select! and the tokio::time::timeout(context.timeouts.stall) wrapper); no test opens a loopback listener that goes quiet mid-stream`
- **Wrong** The path from the Stop button through chat_cancel to the CancelToken to the select! arm in drive_stream is traced by inspection only — no test and no click. The stall guard MEASURED-1 requires (a socket that accepts, streams a few frames and then goes silent) exists only against mocks; nothing wedges a real socket. These are the two failure modes a user actually meets on a slow local model.
- **Done looks like** Send a long turn to the llama.cpp endpoint, click Stop mid-stream, and watch tokens cease immediately, the turn settle as 'Stopped', and the transcript keep the partial answer. Separately, point Vela at a loopback stub that streams three frames then hangs, and watch the turn fail with 'The reply stopped arriving' after the stall budget rather than hanging forever — with a test that asserts it.

#### Context elision and summarisation were never exercised against a genuinely short window

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/src/context.rs (fit_request's drop and summarise paths); the ContextReduced degradation; src/features/conversation/notices.ts:53-61`
- **Wrong** The only live endpoint declares 131,072 tokens, so the drop path, the summarise path and the ContextReduced degradation have only ever been seen in unit tests and mock profiles. The refit-on-overflow half is well guarded (flipping refit_on_context_overflow failed 3 tests), but the ordinary path a long conversation takes on a small local model is unobserved on hardware.
- **Done looks like** Serve a model with a small window (llama.cpp with -c 2048), hold a conversation past it, and watch 'The conversation was shortened' appear under the turn with real dropped-message and token counts, the answer still coherent, and the transcript still showing the earlier messages. Then send a single message larger than the whole window and confirm the 'nothing safe to drop' refusal rather than a silent truncation.

#### Anthropic/Google vendor-specific behaviour is fixture-only and the fixtures are not captures

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/tests/anthropic_fixture_replay.rs and google_fixture_replay.rs; fixtures/anthropic/*.sse, fixtures/google/*.jsonl`
- **Wrong** The replay harness states it plainly: the fixtures are 'transcribed from the published protocol shape — they are not captured traffic, and not one byte of them came from a language model'. So Google's safety-block and thought-signature handling, and Anthropic's redacted-thinking and signature_delta handling, rest on Vela's reading of the docs. Whether either vendor would accept the request bodies Vela builds is entirely untested, and no credential for either exists in this environment. Blocked behind the adapter-construction item.
- **Done looks like** With a real Anthropic key and a real Gemini key, run a capture harness that records actual response bytes into the fixture directories, and confirm the existing replay tests still pass against the captured bytes with no adapter change. Then trigger a real safety block on Gemini and confirm it surfaces as a ProviderError with a FilterVerdict and the 'Refused before the model saw it' sentence.

### Storage, migrations and search

#### Dead arm: MigrationChanged for a recorded-but-unknown version

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/migrations.rs:167-175 (the arm and its comment); :150 validate_sequence; :156-164 the SchemaAhead check; :210 applied() keys the ledger by u32`
- **Wrong** The comment at migrations.rs:168-170 says this branch catches "the file was renamed away or removed". It cannot. Renaming a migration away shrinks MIGRATIONS, so highest_recorded > highest_known and the SchemaAhead check twelve lines earlier returns first with a different error and a different meaning. validate_sequence guarantees `known` is exactly 1..=N and applied() keys by u32, so the only value that can miss `known` is a ledger row at version 0 — external tampering, not a rename. No test covers the arm.
- **Done looks like** Two checks a critic can run. (1) Delete migration 5 from MIGRATIONS, run `cargo test -p vela-store`, and confirm the error surfaced is SchemaAhead, not MigrationChanged — then confirm the comment beside the arm no longer claims otherwise. (2) A named test exists that inserts a schema_migrations row at version 0 and asserts MigrationChanged; deleting the arm makes exactly that test go red. Today deleting the arm breaks nothing.

#### `PRAGMA recursive_triggers = ON` and the reason given for it

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/sqlite.rs:99-100 (comment), :107 (the pragma)`
- **Wrong** The comment says the pragma is what makes the search-index triggers fire for rows removed by ON DELETE CASCADE. It is not. The auditor flipped it to OFF and all 99 tests stayed green, and an independent SQLite 3.45.1 probe with the pragma effective=0 still cleaned the FTS row under the same two-level conversations->messages->message_parts cascade. recursive_triggers only stops a trigger re-entering itself; a trigger invoked from a foreign-key action sub-program is not on its own frame stack. The behaviour is correct, the stated mechanism is wrong, and nothing in the crate distinguishes the pragma being on from off — so its removal would be silent.
- **Done looks like** A critic edits sqlite.rs:107 to `PRAGMA recursive_triggers = OFF;`, runs `cargo test -p vela-store`, and watches a named test go red — a test that reads `PRAGMA recursive_triggers` back and asserts 1, so the claim has something behind it. Alternatively, the comment at :99-100 is rewritten to state the real reason (FK cascade actions run trigger programs regardless) and survives that same flip without lying. Today the flip produces 99 green.

#### `message_parts_search_update` trigger has no write path

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/migrations/0002_message_search.sql:42-55 (the trigger); src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/sqlite.rs:1046 (`DELETE FROM message_parts WHERE message_id = ?1` — how update_message actually works); `git grep "UPDATE message_parts" -- src-tauri/` returns nothing`
- **Wrong** Vela never issues an UPDATE against message_parts. update_message — the streaming-finalise path — replaces parts by DELETE + re-INSERT, which drives the other two triggers. Making the update trigger fully inert leaves all 99 tests green. `editing_a_message_updates_what_search_can_find` reads like the test for this trigger and is not; it exercises the delete/insert pair. The SQL is frozen inside a checksummed migration so it cannot be removed — the risk is purely that someone cites it as coverage.
- **Done looks like** Make the trigger inert (`WHERE rowid = -999` on its DELETE, `WHERE 0` on its INSERT) and run `cargo test -p vela-store`: 99 green is the expected, documented outcome, and a comment at 0002_message_search.sql:42 must say so. Separately, `editing_a_message_updates_what_search_can_find` is renamed or carries a comment naming sqlite.rs:1046 as the mechanism it actually covers, so no reader can mistake it for update-trigger coverage.

### Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus

#### ci.yml's claim that check-transcripts detects a lost .gitattributes (FAIL)

- **Where** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:230-234; scripts/check-transcripts.sh:26-27; .gitattributes:16-18 and :32`
- **Wrong** ci.yml:230-234 says running check-transcripts.sh on Windows "is how we find out if that protection ever stops working". check-transcripts.sh decides with `git diff --quiet -- docs/regression-baseline/mock-matrix`. Measured in a throwaway repo: (A) attribute in force, fresh checkout -> CLEAN; (B) attribute in force, worktree forced to CRLF -> DIRTY, reported; (C) attribute REMOVED, CRLF checkout, file regenerated as LF exactly as record-transcripts.ts writes it -> CLEAN, NOT reported. Case C is the scenario the sentence names and it is invisible, because with core.autocrlf=true and no `-text` git normalises on read so both hash to the LF blob. The real detector is `cargo test --workspace`: CRLF-ifying the seven anthropic .sse fixtures fails control_a/control_d/control_e of the replay suite (12 passed, 3 failed).
- **Done looks like** A critic re-runs case C — remove `*.sse -text`, re-checkout so CR=6, regenerate as LF, run check-transcripts.sh — sees it report CLEAN, and finds ci.yml's comment already told them it would, naming `anthropic_fixture_replay`/`google_fixture_replay` under `cargo test --workspace` as the detector instead. The words "compared byte for byte" must be gone from ci.yml:230, .gitattributes:18 and :32, and from check-transcripts.sh's own exit-0 message, unless a byte comparison is actually added.

The uncovered .txt/.json were measured still line-ending-translated (CR=7 and CR=8). Nothing currently reads those bytes byte-exactly and git's own normalisation makes it harmless for `git diff`, so there is no live defect — but the sentence claims coverage it does not deliver, in the one file whose job is to say exactly which bytes are protected.
- **Done looks like** Either `.gitattributes` also covers the .txt/.json/.tsv transcripts under docs/regression-baseline/ and a CR sweep over mock-matrix returns 0 everywhere, or the comment names the actual scope ("the .sse and .jsonl captures; the .txt/.json transcripts are compared through git and are not byte-protected").
