### Build, packaging and developer experience

#### New-contributor experience — the documented gate is described as something it is not

- **Where** `C:\Users\User\vela-tmp\README.md:14; C:\Users\User\vela-tmp\docs\architecture\conventions.md:356-359 and :362-367; C:\Users\User\vela-tmp\scripts\check-transcripts.sh:12`
- **Wrong** README.md:14 tells every new contributor `pnpm verify` is "the full gate, and a superset of CI — run before every commit". On Windows it cannot complete, and nothing in the README says a POSIX shell is needed. conventions.md:366-367 says verify-covers-ci.test.ts "fails if a gate command appears in the workflow but is unreachable from `verify`, in either direction" — the reverse direction is proven blind twice. check-transcripts.sh:12 says "`pnpm verify` runs it locally" — on Windows verify short-circuits three links earlier and could not invoke the script if it got there.
- **Done looks like** A contributor on a clean Windows machine follows README's four commands top to bottom and hits no failure the README did not warn about. Each of the three sentences is either true (entries 1-4 have landed) or qualified in place with the platform it holds on. Concretely: run `pnpm verify` from a fresh clone on Windows and see it exit 0, or see the README sentence that predicted the exact failure you got.


### Scheduler, dual local endpoint, memory, canvas/artifacts

#### A user-facing way to turn the endpoint on / Endpoint bearer auth and empty-key refusal

- **Where** `docs/ and README.md (no VELA_LOCAL_ENDPOINT mention anywhere); src-tauri/crates/vela-endpoint/src/route.rs:143 and its test the_anthropic_sdk_key_header_is_not_a_second_way_in`
- **Wrong** Two undocumented facts a user will hit immediately. (1) The five environment variables that are today the only way to run the endpoint appear in no doc, README line or script. (2) x-api-key is deliberately not accepted, so a stock Anthropic SDK client configured with ANTHROPIC_API_KEY gets a 401 and no explanation; it must send Authorization: Bearer. That interoperability constraint is written only in a Rust test name.
- **Done looks like** A docs page (or README section) that a reader can follow start-to-finish to get a turn out of the endpoint: the variables or the settings row, the two base URLs (http://host:port for Anthropic, http://host:port/v1 for OpenAI), the Bearer-not-x-api-key requirement stated as a numbered gotcha, and the wildcard-bind tool-policy rule. Acceptance: hand it to someone who has not read the source and watch them get a completion without asking a question.

### Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest

#### False comment: debuglog.rs claims everything it records is post-scrubber

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/src/debuglog.rs:35-41`
- **Wrong** The '# What is written' section says the bytes are 'as UpstreamBytes holds them, which means **after** the byte scrubber has run' and concludes 'the credential is already gone from those bytes before this module can see them, so an opt-in debug log is not a way to opt into leaking your own API key into a file on disk.' At HEAD there are six production record sites and only two carry UpstreamBytes (http.rs:442, http.rs:480). http.rs:1177 records `error.without_url().to_string()`, http.rs:1147 records `refused.to_string()`, and stream.rs:131 / anthropic/stream.rs:179 / google/stream.rs:176 record `frame.to_string()`. The two http.rs cases are safe by CONSTRUCTION — `without_url()` removes the only place a query credential could be, and the redirect line is built from `authority_of()` (scheme + host + port) — not by any scrubber. A reviewer auditing this file for the scrubber guarantee is misled about where the safety actually comes from, which is the same class of defect as app-runtime.ts:20.
- **Done looks like** The section enumerates each production record site and names the barrier that applies to it (UpstreamBytes scrubbing / `without_url()` / authority-only text / decoded-and-scrubbed frames). Test the fix by asking whether a reviewer adding a seventh `debuglog::record` call with raw endpoint text could tell from that paragraph that they owe a justification. Today they cannot.

#### False comment: redact.rs names the wrong load-bearing mechanism and miscounts its own call sites

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/src/redact.rs:20 and :24-25`
- **Wrong** Two claims, both wrong at HEAD. (a) 'Two mechanisms close it, and the first is the one that matters' — gutting `RequestUrl::redacted()` to return the wire form left all 34 integration canaries green; what actually keeps a credential out of a rendered error is a THIRD mechanism these docs never name: `ProviderError` has no field that can hold endpoint- or client-supplied text at all, because `map_reqwest_error` (http.rs:1174-1179) emits a typed `TransportFailure` + `Diagnosis` and sends reqwest's own string, with `.without_url()` applied, to the debug log instead. Anyone reasoning from this paragraph would protect the wrong thing. (b) 'there is exactly one production call site' for `RequestUrl::expose` — `git grep '\.url\.expose()'` returns two, http.rs:1039 and http.rs:1040. They are the Get and Post arms of one match expression, so the adjacent comment at http.rs:1036 ('THE ONE PLACE') is the defensible phrasing; the redact.rs sentence is offered as a grep-ability guarantee and does not survive the grep.
- **Done looks like** The docs name the typed error surface as the primary mechanism and demote both redaction layers to belts, and the call-site count either says two or says 'one expression, both arms'. Verify by running the grep the comment invites and getting the number the comment claims.

#### False comment: the secrets_get test is called a 'structural guard' and is a single string match

- **Where** `src-tauri/src/ipc/mod.rs:224-225 (comment above `no_command_returns_secret_material`, whose body is `assert!(!COMMAND_ALLOWLIST.contains(&"secrets_get"))` at :226-230)`
- **Wrong** The comment reads 'Structural guard for the one-way rule. `secrets_get` must never appear; anything needing a secret value runs in Rust.' It is one literal string comparison. A command named `secrets_read`, `secrets_reveal` or `settings_get_credential` sails past it unchanged. The actual structural guard is that `vela_core::secret::SecretValue` derives `Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Deserialize` and deliberately NOT `Serialize` (src-tauri/crates/vela-core/src/secret.rs:39-41), so no such command could compile. Calling the string match structural invites a reviewer to trust it for a case it does not cover — and the same overstated framing is mirrored in src/platform/contract.ts:31 and src/platform/contract.test.ts:85.
- **Done looks like** The comment says the test is a reminder and names SecretValue's missing `Serialize` as the guard that actually holds. Better: strengthen the assertion so the comment becomes true — no allowlisted name matching `secrets_(get\|read\|reveal\|export\|show)` — and verify by adding `secrets_reveal` to COMMAND_ALLOWLIST and watching the test go red, which it does not today.

#### False framing: vela-store still asserts the per-user directory that private_fs.rs was written to demolish

- **Where** `src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/location.rs:4-5 and src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/lib.rs:25-26; contrast with src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/src/private_fs.rs:15-40 and the correction debuglog.rs:144-158 already carries`
- **Wrong** private_fs.rs exists because 'the application-data directory is already per-user, and nothing here widens it' was measured false on real Windows. Both vela-store docs still describe the target as 'the real per-user application-data directory' with no qualifier, and location.rs:65 creates it with a bare `create_dir_all`. The resolution claim is literally true; the security implication a reader takes from it is contradicted by measurement on this machine (vela.db: Protected=False, CodexSandboxUsers ReadAndExecute inherited=True). debuglog.rs:144-158 carries an explicit 'what this used to say, and why it was wrong' correction for the identical sentence; these two files do not, so the demolished assumption is still standing exactly where the larger body of prompts and answers lives.
- **Done looks like** location.rs and lib.rs say that per-user describes where the OS puts the directory and is not a statement about who can read it, and point at whatever hardens it once the DACL fix lands. Acceptance: a reviewer reading only location.rs must not be able to conclude vela.db is owner-only. Pair the doc change with the FIX entry — landing the doc alone would be worse, since it would document a gap nobody closed.

## PASS rows dropping to UNVERIFIED under the supreme rule (173)

- **Storage, migrations and search** — Migration ledger + checksum tamper-evidence — mutation-proven inside `cargo test`, but no auditor has ever watched a real %APPDATA% database be refused in a running window. Under §4 that refusal is invisible, so the reaches-user half is not merely untested, it is probably false.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — Existing-database in-place upgrade against a real file — the tempdir upgrade is genuinely mutation-proven (durability.rs:176, left:0 right:1), but no one has taken a real pre-0005 vela.db from %APPDATA% and watched an installed Vela open it.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — Forward-only contiguity check (validate_sequence) runs before any SQL — read only, explicitly not mutated, never observed at startup.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — Per-migration transaction: a failure leaves the previous version intact — test unproven, not mutated, never observed on a real user database.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — SchemaAhead: a database from a newer Vela is refused, not downgraded — test unproven against a synthetic ledger row; the user-facing consequence of the refusal is the FAIL in item 1, so the reaches-user grade rests on a path that terminates in nothing visible.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — FTS index is cleaned when a conversation is deleted — the store mechanism bites under mutation, and the Desktop-shell auditor did delete a conversation in the running window, but nobody inspected message_search afterwards. The cleanup itself is traced, not observed.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — Malformed search input is a user error, and orphan index rows cannot become phantom hits — read only. Nobody has typed FTS5 operator syntax into the command palette in a running window and watched it come back as a user error rather than a crash.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — Title search treats LIKE metacharacters literally — test unproven and unmutated. Nobody has typed `100%` into the palette and confirmed it does not match `1000 tokens`.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — Foreign-key enforcement verified on open — mutation-proven in the crate, but the reaches-user claim is that a real launch performs the check against the real database; that has been inferred from the app launching, not observed.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — WAL journalling: enabled, verified on open, sidecar on disk — the sidecar assertion is against a tempdir. Nobody has looked in %APPDATA%\\dev.vela.desktop for vela.db-wal. The auditor's own could-not-establish list names this directory as untested.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — Durability under an unclean shutdown (hot WAL recovery) — this one is the clearest. The measurement went through Python's SQLite 3.45.1 executing Vela's DDL, not through SqliteStore::open. Vela's own Rust has never been shown to recover a hot WAL, and the crate ships no unclean-shutdown test at all.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — Credential-free schema — two real guards, both green, neither mutated, and the reaches-user grade is a trace.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — Schedules and their run history survive a restart — regraded to not-wired; see the backlog item. No user can produce the state this proves survives.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — Default project seeded by a migration — regraded to not-wired; see the backlog item. The seed exists on disk and nothing a user does references it.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — NOT regraded, and I want the asymmetry on the record: 'FTS search reaches a user surface' survives PASS, because a different auditor (Desktop shell, REPORT.md:204) typed 'kitchen' into the command palette in a running window and got host hits labelled 'In message' / 'In thinking' — that is store_search driving search_messages against the FTS5 index, clicked and watched. Also not regraded: the two `n/a` rows (the no_two_shipped_migrations_share_a_version_or_a_body guard, and 'migration unit tests are in-memory only'), because their subject is the test suite itself and the auditor broke it and watched it go red — there is nothing to click.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — All twelve PASS rows in this domain drop to UNVERIFIED. The auditor states plainly that they did not launch the app, so not one aspect in Projects, Skills or MCP was clicked in a running window. Enumerated below, hardest cases first.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — Reparse-point-aware delete (vela_projects::remove_tree) — graded PASS / test bites / reaches-user. 'Reaches-user' rests entirely on the claim that probe_link_strategy's scratch cleanup runs at every launch. That is a traced path through src-tauri/src/lib.rs setup, not an observation; nobody watched a launch.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — Skill mount is a junction on Windows, never a symlink — PASS / hardware / reaches-user. The reparse tag 0xA0000003 was read back off a real tempfile::TempDir by a cargo test, which is hardware for the crate but not for the product. The 'reaches-user' half — that probe_link_strategy creates and removes a real junction under the real %APPDATA% at every launch — was never observed, and the auditor lists exactly this as something they could not establish.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — Canonical skill store created empty at launch before anything reads it — PASS / test unproven / reaches-user. Traced from SkillsHandle::under_data_dir and ProjectHost::establish in lib.rs setup; the named test is green but was not mutated, and no launch was watched.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — Project CRUD command layer (create/get/list/update/delete/move) — PASS / not-wired. Cannot be PASS: no renderer reaches it.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — The case-folding backstop asks the volume, not the case table — PASS / not-wired.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — Colliding enabledSkills refused at the command layer — PASS / not-wired.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — Working directory: read, never created, never deleted — PASS / test unproven / not-wired. Doubly weak: unreachable and unmutated.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — SKILL.md store: frontmatter parsing and progressive disclosure — PASS / not-wired. The assembled-app gate test is the strongest evidence in this domain and it still is not a window.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — MCP stdio transport, end to end — PASS / not-wired.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — MCP child process environment isolation — PASS / not-wired.

- **Conversation surface** — All 18 PASS rows in this domain drop to UNVERIFIED. The domain's own auditor states it outright in REPORT.md:177 — 'Nothing in this domain was exercised against a real window or a real endpoint — I was not the auditor authorised to launch the app, and I did not query the llama.cpp server on 8033. Every rendering claim above is jsdom (VERIFIED-BY-FAKE in this repo's vocabulary), including the three FAIL findings.' Not one aspect here was clicked in a running window. The 18 are listed individually below.
- **Conversation surface** — Compose & send a turn (composer → chat_send payload) — mutation-proven in jsdom; never sent through a real window to a real endpoint.
- **Conversation surface** — Composer keyboard contract (Enter, Shift+Enter, IME) — synthetic jsdom events only; the real WebView2 event sequence for a Japanese/Chinese/Korean candidate accept was never observed, and only the Escape branch was mutated.
- **Conversation surface** — Cancel: Escape and the Stop button reach the host — mutation-proven at the hook/DOM level in jsdom; chat_cancel was never pressed against a live turn.
- **Conversation surface** — Cancelling reads as 'you stopped this', not as a failure — jsdom render assertion.
- **Conversation surface** — Streaming: first token committed synchronously, later frames coalesced per frame — the mechanism is mutation-proven; the ~16ms claim in the header is unmeasured and no real stream was watched paint.
- **Conversation surface** — Reasoning kept out of the answer channel (renderer-side guard) — 13 tests bite, all jsdom; no real reasoning model was streamed into the window.
- **Conversation surface** — Reasoning is never replayed back to the endpoint — proven against a fake host; no real second turn was ever inspected on the wire.
- **Conversation surface** — Markdown: model text is rendered, never built into HTML — behavioural but jsdom; the hostile payload was never rendered in WebView2, which is where an XSS would actually matter.
- **Conversation surface** — Rendered errors carry no endpoint-authored text — strongest evidence in the domain (mutation + Rust source on both sides), still never observed as a real error from a real endpoint in the window.
- **Conversation surface** — The error surface is still actionable (title, Vela's sentence, endpoint, trace, retry) — the 'Could not reach the endpoint' string came from executing the component in jsdom, not from an unreachable endpoint in a running app.
- **Conversation surface** — A turn the host refused is drawn as Vela's own fault, not the endpoint's — jsdom; and see the agent-path backlog item, where the same box's deletion went unnoticed by 15 tests.
- **Conversation surface** — Retry of the last turn: no duplicate message, store rows replaced — two mutations bite, both jsdom, against a fake store.
- **Conversation surface** — Transcript restore: no double-write, no spinner that cannot resolve — jsdom over a fake transcript; never verified by reopening a real conversation from vela.db.
- **Conversation surface** — An unreadable transcript says so and blocks sending — the unreadable state was injected, never produced by a real store failure.
- **Conversation surface** — Empty state names the model's capabilities from the struct alone — read, not mutated, not clicked.
- **Conversation surface** — Degradation notices: wording, totality, and reachability — totality is a typecheck, but reachability is false for at least two arms; see the backlog item.
- **Conversation surface** — Agent path: failure sentences vs failure rendering — graded PASS on evidence that actively shows the rendering is unguarded; see the backlog item.
- **Conversation surface** — Scroll: follow the stream only while pinned to the bottom — the plumbing is explicitly unverifiable in jsdom and the file says so; see the backlog item.

Note the runtime auditor independently saw this same assertion fail twice on an unmutated tree under load (REPORT.md:153) and graded it UNVERIFIED in their domain; two auditors disagree on the same joint, and the one who saw it flake is the one who ran it more. UNVERIFIED, and possibly a real race in useConversation's async memory read.
- **Scheduler, dual local endpoint, memory, canvas/artifacts** — Memory scope isolation (project vs global) at the store — mutation bit in sqlite tests. The project half of the partition it protects is unreachable from any UI, so what is proven is that a partition nobody can cross is not crossed. UNVERIFIED.
- **Scheduler, dual local endpoint, memory, canvas/artifacts** — Past-chat search — test unproven, not mutated, traced CommandPalette.tsx:114 → use-conversations.ts:170 → repository.search. Classic traced path. UNVERIFIED.
- **Scheduler, dual local endpoint, memory, canvas/artifacts** — Canvas frame boundary: opaque origin, CSP, no frame before accepted — the best-guarded rule in the domain (8 tests on one mutation) and still jsdom: an iframe sandbox attribute asserted in jsdom is not an iframe a browser refused to give an origin to. UNVERIFIED until watched in a real WebView2.
- **Scheduler, dual local endpoint, memory, canvas/artifacts** — Canvas artifact detection, versioning, version rail and diff — canvas-wiring.test.tsx drives App level in jsdom. No one has asked a model for an artifact in the running window and clicked the version rail. UNVERIFIED.
- **Scheduler, dual local endpoint, memory, canvas/artifacts** — Canvas refuses languages it cannot draw and still shows source — test unproven, plus the latent frameFor arms. UNVERIFIED.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — A credential never appears in a rendered error — the flagship promise of this domain. 34 canaries pass against real ReqwestTransport and real broken loopback sockets, and the typed-error argument is sound, but every one of them is a Rust-level test. Nobody drove a real failed turn in a running window and read the error text on screen. UNVERIFIED.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — Renderer credential surface is write-only and reaches a real UI — pure trace: ModelWorkspace.tsx:209 -> use-providers.ts:95 -> settings-repository.ts:70 -> adapter.invoke('secrets_set') -> lib.rs:222. Exactly the traced-call-path shape FINDING 1 says must not be PASS. Nobody typed a key into the running app. UNVERIFIED.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — `KeyringStore` is the live backend in the shipped binary — read off Cargo.toml:105 and state.rs:58 plus the presence of libkeyring-*.rlib in target/debug/deps. The shipped binary was never launched, no test constructs a KeyringStore, and `cmdkey /list` shows no dev.vela.desktop entry. UNVERIFIED.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — Debug log is off at every launch — the auditor states plainly it is PASS 'on inspection, not on a test' and that they were not authorised to start the app. Every ipc::diagnostics test calls disable() first, so it asserts the state it just set. UNVERIFIED.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — `app_info.secretBackend` is honest about a fake — traced to HomeSurface.tsx:160-162 and browser-adapter.ts:989. My domain's evidence is read-only. (The desktop-shell auditor claims a hardware sighting of 'Bridge ready · os-keychain' in the status bar, REPORT.md:213 — if that was a real running window it partially rescues this row, but not on my evidence.) UNVERIFIED.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — Secret material escaping the `SecretValue` wrapper — the PASS rests on 'I found no production reader that turns HttpRequest.headers into a string.' That is a search, not a guard. Absence of a caller today is the same evidence class that graded Projects PASS. UNVERIFIED.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — Query-credential URL redaction (`RequestUrl::redacted`) — the mutation survived all 34 integration canaries and was caught only by two unit tests in the same file it protects. The auditor called it a test-cover gap; under the supreme rule the row itself is UNVERIFIED at the integration level.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — Scrubber second barrier (decode-then-scrub) — bites hard at the Rust level (8 lib tests plus two canary tests), but the row claims reaches-user and no scrubbed string was ever observed in rendered UI. Related: `scrub_bytes` / `hold_back_len` was not mutated at all. UNVERIFIED as a reaches-user claim.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — No credential lands in the SQLite database — a real test against a real file with a genuinely non-vacuous control, but not mutated, and the file it greps is a tempfile, not %APPDATA%\dev.vela.desktop\vela.db. Nobody grepped the live 315 KB database on this machine for a canary. UNVERIFIED.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — Diagnostics directory is owner-only on Windows — this is the uncomfortable one. The Get-Acl reading is real and current, but that directory was created by a PRIOR launch of the app at an earlier commit, not by a run anyone drove at HEAD. It is a fossil that proves the code worked once, somewhere. Nobody at HEAD clicked the Debug Log switch and watched a fresh DACL land. UNVERIFIED at HEAD.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — `Auth::None` never touches the keychain; no empty auth header — graded 'test unproven'. The ExplodingStore construction makes it close to self-proving, which is why the auditor did not mutate it, but 'passes' is not 'bites'. UNVERIFIED.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — `SecretValue` is not `Serialize` — read only. I would defend this one hardest: it is compiler-enforced, and a serialising command genuinely would not build. But the evidence recorded is a derive list read off secret.rs:39-41, nobody tried to add a `Serialize` and watch the build break, and the rule is the rule. UNVERIFIED, and the cheapest of these to convert back to PASS.
- **Providers and model layer** — ALL 17 PASS rows in this domain regrade to UNVERIFIED. The auditor never launched the app — §1 of docs/audit/providers.md records only `cargo test -p vela-providers`, `npx vitest run`, and Rust test binaries driving the llama.cpp server on :8033. Not one click happened in a running Vela window. The list below is therefore complete, not selective, and is ordered worst-evidence first.
- **Providers and model layer** — Error taxonomy and the typed, closed Diagnosis — evidence 'test unproven': the in-file control was read, never run against a planted defect. Nothing was broken and nothing was clicked.
- **Providers and model layer** — Egress control: no ambient proxy, no redirect, Auth::None sends nothing — evidence 'test unproven': three test files passed in a baseline run and were never mutated.
- **Providers and model layer** — Provider composition root (configured → registered → resolvable) — pure trace: lib.rs:86 → ipc/settings.rs:148/:163 → ipc/chat.rs::resolve_provider. Exactly the command-registered → IPC → repository → component shape the supreme rule names as `traced`. Nobody added an endpoint in the window and watched a turn answer.
- **Providers and model layer** — Opt-in local debug log and its private directory — pure trace: generate_handler! → App.tsx → ModelWorkspace → EndpointsPanel:138 → DebugLogSwitch. The switch was never toggled and no log file was ever opened.
- **Providers and model layer** — Tool-call emulation for models with no native tools — regrades past UNVERIFIED to FAIL: I traced the gate at use-conversation.ts:610 and it closes on exactly the models emulation serves, so this is not merely unclicked, it is unreachable. Filed as the first backlog item.
- **Providers and model layer** — Structured-output validation (MEASURED-5) — the probe measured it on hardware, but no user path can request it (build_request never sets response_format). 'reaches-user' is wrong on its face.
- **Providers and model layer** — Shipped adapter (CompatProvider) driving a real endpoint — the strongest evidence in the domain, and still a Rust test binary constructing CompatProvider by hand with the five arguments ProviderHost::build passes. Not the shipped composition root, not the window.
- **Providers and model layer** — SSE decoding and stream normalisation (MEASURED-1, MEASURED-2) — same live turn, same caveat: measured through a hand-built provider, not through chat_send and the chat:event channel into the transcript.
- **Providers and model layer** — Capability probing (probe evidence, never a provider-name table) — the repo's own #[ignore]d probe against :8033 via cargo, not the Check button in the model workspace.
- **Providers and model layer** — Server discovery, flavour detection and error-dialect normalisation — the llama.cpp arm confirmed live via the same Rust binary; vLLM, Ollama and OpenAI-shaped dialects are mock-only.
- **Providers and model layer** — Reasoning channel separated across frame boundaries (MEASURED-3) — strongest mutation result in the repo (7 tests across all three adapters), but the assertion that no reasoning reaches the answer *on screen* was never checked in a rendered transcript.
- **Providers and model layer** — Deliberation is not an instruction (the AnswerChannel chokepoint) — mutation bit at the crate level; the security property (a model thinking about delete_everything must not thereby call it) was never observed end to end through the agent runtime in the window.
- **Providers and model layer** — Native tool-call accumulation and the two wire shapes (MEASURED-4 / FINDING 1) — mutation bit, in exactly one integration file, and no tool call was ever watched arriving in the UI.
- **Providers and model layer** — Context-window accounting, fitting, and refit-on-overflow (MEASURED-7) — mutation bit against mocks; never seen against a genuinely short window, and the 'The conversation was shortened' notice was never rendered.
- **Providers and model layer** — Credential redaction (RequestUrl and Scrubber) — mutation bit on 5 tests; no error was ever rendered in the window and inspected for credential material.
- **Providers and model layer** — Model-agnosticism: adding a provider needs zero renderer change — a source-scanning test, which is the one kind of guard a click could not strengthen; still, it was graded on a vitest run, not on the window, and its top-level scan can pass vacuously (see backlog).
- **Providers and model layer** — Rust↔TypeScript parity of the provider wire types — same: a source-reading test, graded on a vitest run. Its parser has a known blind spot (per-variant serde(rename)).

## False comments re-derived against HEAD (101)

A comment is not evidence. Each of these was checked against `src/platform/contract.ts`, `src-tauri/src/ipc/mod.rs` and the tree as it stands.

- **Storage, migrations and search** — src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/sqlite.rs:99-100 — "recursive_triggers: the search-index triggers must also fire for rows removed by ON DELETE CASCADE." FALSE. What is actually true: SQLite runs a grandchild's AFTER DELETE trigger under a foreign-key CASCADE action regardless of this pragma; recursive_triggers only prevents a trigger from re-entering itself. Evidence at HEAD: flipping :107 to OFF leaves all 99 vela-store tests green, and an independent SQLite 3.45.1 probe with the pragma effective=0 still cleaned the FTS row through the same two-level cascade. Nothing in the crate distinguishes the pragma's two values, so the comment names a guard that is doing no work.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/migrations.rs:168-170 — "Recorded, known-version range, but absent from this build's list: the file was renamed away or removed." FALSE. What is actually true: renaming a migration away shrinks MIGRATIONS, so highest_recorded > highest_known and the SchemaAhead check at migrations.rs:156-164 returns first, with a different error and a different meaning; renaming one out of the middle fails validate_sequence (migrations.rs:150) before any of this runs. Since validate_sequence guarantees `known` is exactly 1..=N and applied() keys the ledger by u32 (migrations.rs:210), the only value that can miss `known` is a ledger row at version 0 — external tampering. The comment describes a mechanism that cannot operate.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — src-tauri/src/store_host.rs:49-52 — "Failing here aborts startup on purpose. A chat client that silently runs with no system of record would accept the user's work and drop it; refusing to start is the honest failure." FALSE in its load-bearing word. What is actually true at HEAD: it aborts, but not honestly. main.rs:2 sets windows_subsystem = "windows" in release, so the panic from lib.rs:251's .expect("error while running Vela") goes to a stderr with no console, and DebugLogHandle is not managed until lib.rs:97 — twenty-seven lines after store_host::open at lib.rs:70, inside the same setup closure — so the opt-in debug log does not exist yet either. The user gets no window, no dialog and no log. The comment claims a virtue the code does not deliver.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/sqlite.rs:9-11 — "A desktop client has a single user issuing a handful of writes a minute; a pool would add lifetime and lock-ordering complexity to solve contention that does not exist." STALE at HEAD. What is actually true: src-tauri/src/lib.rs:166-167 unconditionally calls scheduler_host::reap_on_boot and ::spawn, and scheduler_host.rs:72-88 starts a named thread that calls vela_store::poll_once against the same Arc<dyn VelaStore> every 30 seconds — a second in-process writer (schedule fires create conversations and messages) that no user drives. The Mutex still serialises correctly so there is no defect, but the design rationale's premise is no longer true, and the crate's own busy_timeout comment twelve lines below (sqlite.rs:104-105, "another Vela window ... should wait, not fail") already contradicts it.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — NOT false, checked and cleared, so nobody re-opens them: vela-store/src/lib.rs:17 cites `SqliteStore::journal_mode` as the WAL verification and that method really exists (sqlite.rs:157-160); vela-store/src/scheduler.rs:35-37's "finish_schedule_run exists, is tested, and has no caller outside tests" is exactly true at HEAD (git grep confirms every hit is a trait decl, the impl, five scheduler.rs tests and durability.rs:413); src-tauri/src/ipc/memory.rs:9-16's "nothing calls these commands from a turn" is true — memory_add's only shipped caller is use-memory.ts:105, which a user drives; and 0002_message_search.sql:7-8's "a write path that forgets to reindex cannot exist" holds, because INSERT, DELETE and UPDATE on message_parts are all covered by a trigger.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — src/runtime/app-runtime.ts:19-21 — 'COMMAND_ALLOWLIST in src/platform/contract.ts has no command that reads a project, so there is nothing to call.' FALSE. contract.ts:1650-1657 carries eight project_* commands, including project_get (:1652) and project_list (:1654); all eight are in generate_handler! at src-tauri/src/lib.rs:202-209 and answered by BrowserAdapter at browser-adapter.ts:826-843. This sentence is the stated justification for the `readProjectInstructions: () => Promise.resolve(null)` stub eight lines below it at :83.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — src/runtime/project-context.ts:11-13 — 'src/platform/contract.ts has no project_get in its allowlist either, so there is no host call to make yet.' FALSE. project_get is literally at src/platform/contract.ts:1652 and registered at src-tauri/src/lib.rs:204.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — src/runtime/project-context.ts:4-7 — 'COMMAND_ALLOWLIST has nothing for skills or memory, and no command reads a skill's *body*.' FALSE on both halves. skills_list (contract.ts:1676) and skills_read (:1677) are allowlisted and registered (lib.rs:228-229), and skills_read returns the instruction body — src-tauri/src/ipc/skills.rs's own header calls it 'level two and three for exactly one skill: the instruction body'. Five memory_* commands sit at contract.ts:1642-1646.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — src/platform/contract-harness.ts:738-740 — '**Today there are none**: COMMAND_ALLOWLIST has nothing for skills or memory.' FALSE, same evidence as above. Worse, the file contradicts itself inside one comment block: eight lines later at :746-748 it says project instructions resolve 'through project_get in src/platform/contract-project.ts'. Two adjacent paragraphs disagree about whether project_get exists.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — src-tauri/src/ipc/skills.rs:21-23 — 'Enabling a skill *into* a project is vela_skills::mount, and no command calls it — see that crate's header for why (there is no projects table, and project_update does not exist).' The conclusion is true; both parenthesised reasons are false. The projects table is created by src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/migrations/0001_initial_schema.sql:11 (and migrations.rs:312 names it), and project_update is allowlisted at contract.ts:1657 and registered at lib.rs:209. src/platform/project-host-parity.test.ts:15-17 then cites this comment as its own premise.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — src-tauri/crates/vela-skills/src/lib.rs:24-30 — 'the commands that would call them (project_create, project_update, project_reconcile_skills) do not exist: src/platform/contract-project.ts declares them, registers none, and there is no projects table for them to read.' FALSE on all three counts. All three are in COMMAND_ALLOWLIST (contract.ts:1650, :1657, :1656) and in generate_handler! (lib.rs:202, 209, 208); the projects table has existed since migration 0001. The true reason mount and enablement are dead: those three commands were wired to a different implementation, vela-projects.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — src/data/skills-repository.ts:26-28 — 'Enabling a skill into a project is the mount, which lives in src-tauri/crates/vela-skills/src/mount.rs and has no command in front of it yet.' FALSE and actively misdirecting. The mount that runs is vela-projects (src-tauri/src/ipc/project.rs is the only file under src-tauri/src that names vela_projects), and project_reconcile_skills is precisely the command in front of it — ipc/project.rs:547 says it is 'the same operation under a second name' as project_layout. This comment points a future implementer at the dead crate.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — src/data/mcp-repository.ts:22-24 — 'Calling a tool is the harness contract's ToolExecutor, which is not built — see the report for this track.' FALSE. ToolExecutor is built and on the shipping path: src/runtime/app-runtime.ts:61 builds createSubagentToolkit(...) and :80-81 supplies `toolsFor: (request, runs) => toolkit.toolsFor(request, runs)` to createHarnessRuntime, so every admitted run gets one. What is genuinely absent is an mcp_call_tool command — `git grep -n 'mcp_call_tool' -- src src-tauri` is empty at HEAD — so no executor can dispatch an MCP tool. The comment blames the wrong missing piece.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — src/app/App.tsx:92-93 — 'DEFAULT_PROJECT_ID is passed literally … The project feature does not exist, so there is exactly one project and this is its id.' False as written. The project feature exists host-side: a projects table since migration 0001, eight registered commands, a private workspace created at project_create and repaired at every project_layout, a skills mount, and a full BrowserAdapter double. What does not exist is a renderer surface. The precise sentence is 'no module under src/ calls a project command, so there is nothing here to select from' — and that distinction is exactly what separates a BUILD item from a WIRE item in this backlog.
- **Conversation surface** — src/data/mcp-repository.ts:22-23 — 'It does not execute anything. Calling a tool is the harness contract's `ToolExecutor`, which is not built — see the report for this track.' FALSE at HEAD. A ToolExecutor is built and runs on every agent turn: src/runtime/subagent-toolkit.ts:156 `toolsFor(request, runs): ToolExecutor`, wired at src/runtime/app-runtime.ts:81-82 into createHarnessRuntime, placed on every run's services at src/runtime/harness-runtime.ts:116, and invoked at src/runtime/agent-loop-harness.ts:430 `this.services.tools.execute(call, this.abort.signal)` — reached from the composition root at src/app/App.tsx:119 `createAgentRuntime(adapter)`. What is actually true is narrower and worse: no executor executes MCP tools, and nothing in the product imports this file at all — the only callers of createMcpRepository, toolCatalogue and unavailableServers anywhere in src/ are in src/data/mcp-repository.test.ts. The comment blames an unbuilt abstraction for what is in fact a missing import.
- **Conversation surface** — src/features/conversation/tool-calls.ts:108-112 — 'Correlation ids currently executing. Empty on every build that has no tool runtime — which is why `running` can never be claimed by accident.' The premise is false at HEAD: this build HAS a tool runtime (App.tsx:119 → harness-runtime.ts:116 → agent-loop-harness.ts:430). `running` is empty because src/features/conversation/MessageTurn.tsx:74 renders `<ToolCalls outcomes={turn.outcomes} progress={turn.toolProgress} />` and passes neither prop, and because use-conversation.ts:773-798 drops every run event that is not `chat` or `runFinished`. The comment reads as a safety property (nothing can claim `running` because nothing runs tools) when the real state is an unwired prop on a shipping build that does run tools.
- **Conversation surface** — src/features/conversation/TurnNotices.tsx:52 — 'Correlation ids currently executing. Empty until Vela runs tools.' Same falsehood, restated at the transcript's entry point. Vela runs tools today on the agent path (agent-loop-harness.ts:430). The prop is empty because MessageTurn.tsx:74 never supplies it, not because the feature is unbuilt — and this is the comment a future reader hits first, so it is the one most likely to stop someone from wiring it.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — src/runtime/app-runtime.ts:20 — '`COMMAND_ALLOWLIST` in `src/platform/contract.ts` has no command that reads a project, so there is nothing to call.' FALSE. contract.ts:1650-1657 lists project_create, project_delete, project_get, project_layout, project_list, project_move_conversation, project_reconcile_skills, project_update — eight commands, declared at contract.ts:1584-1591. `project_get` returns ProjectRes whose ProjectView carries `readonly instructions: string` (contract-project.ts:375). This false sentence is the stated reason readProjectInstructions is hardcoded to null at app-runtime.ts:83.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — src/runtime/project-context.ts:12 — '`src/platform/contract.ts` has no `project_get` in its allowlist either, so there is no host call to make yet.' FALSE. `project_get` is declared at contract.ts:1586 and allowlisted at contract.ts:1652.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — src/runtime/project-context.ts:5 — '`COMMAND_ALLOWLIST` has nothing for skills or memory'. FALSE. contract.ts:1676-1677 allowlist skills_list and skills_read; contract.ts:1642-1646 allowlist memory_add, memory_clear_scope, memory_delete, memory_list, memory_update.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — src/runtime/project-context.ts:6 — 'no command reads a skill''s *body*, so a resolver cannot serve that source yet and must not pretend to by returning refs it cannot load'. FALSE. SkillsReadRes (contract.ts:1383-1391) has `readonly body: string`, documented at contract.ts:1386 as 'The instruction text: loaded because this skill was asked for.' The `skill` arm of the resolver is buildable at HEAD.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — src/features/conversation/use-conversation.ts:706 — 'With no project command in the allowlist this is empty today'. FALSE for the same reason as app-runtime.ts:20. What is actually true: `preload` is empty because app-runtime.ts:83 hands the resolver a reader that resolves null, not because the command is missing. This is the second site repeating the same falsehood, at the call site rather than the seam.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — src/platform/contract-harness.ts:739 — '**Today there are none**: `COMMAND_ALLOWLIST` has nothing for skills or memory'. FALSE at HEAD, and this is the sentence project-context.ts:4-7 quotes as its authority — one wrong sentence in a frozen contract propagating into two runtime files.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — src/platform/contract-harness.ts:753 — 'no command reads a skill''s *body*, so a resolver cannot serve that source yet and must not pretend to by returning refs it cannot load'. FALSE, per SkillsReadRes.body. Note the same paragraph (contract-harness.ts:745-750) is CORRECT and useful: it names project_get as the route for projectInstructions — so the contract simultaneously tells a builder the command exists and that it does not.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — src/platform/contract-harness.ts:1495 — 'The in-flight check a conversation view makes on mount, before subscribing.' FALSE as a description of the product. `git grep forConversation -- src/` at HEAD returns contract-harness.ts:1480 and :1496, live-runs.ts:28 and :312, and live-runs.test.ts / subagent-toolkit.test.ts. No conversation view makes this check; that is the first backlog entry.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — src/runtime/project-context.test.ts:55-56 — the test 'serves only the sources it can actually load' quotes 'no command reads a skill''s body, so a resolver cannot serve that source yet' as its stated premise. The assertion it makes is still true of the code; its reason is false at HEAD, so the test now documents a constraint that no longer exists.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — src/runtime/agent-loop-harness.ts:345-347 — 'every test in this directory passed, because they all use a transcript double that accepts anything — `adapter-integration.test.ts` is what found it.' STALE/FALSE at HEAD in the good direction: the EMPTY_TURN_PARTS → [] mutation now also fails agent-loop-harness.test.ts:667 ('writes the transcript as the run goes, not at the end'), which is in this directory.
- **Desktop shell, layout and visual design** — src/app/App.tsx:93 — 'The project feature does not exist, so there is exactly one project and this is its id'. False about the host, and it is the stated reason DEFAULT_PROJECT_ID is passed as a literal. At HEAD src/platform/contract.ts:1584-1591 and 1650-1657 carry eight project_* commands (create, delete, get, layout, list, move_conversation, reconcile_skills, update) and src-tauri/src/ipc/project.rs implements them. What is actually true: no renderer surface and no src/data project repository exist — `git ls-files src/data \| grep -i project` returns nothing.
- **Desktop shell, layout and visual design** — src/platform/contract.ts:272-274 — 'the field exists so the UI can show the user telemetry is off rather than assert it'. False at HEAD: `git grep telemetryEnabled -- src` returns only contract.ts:276, browser-adapter.ts:1062, settings-repository.ts:17 and tests — no renderer reads it. HomeSurface.tsx:167 asserts 'off — there is no command that can turn it on' as a literal and AppShell.tsx:63 asserts 'Offline · no telemetry' as a literal. The same false premise is repeated in src-tauri/src/ipc/settings.rs:7-8 ('settings_get reports telemetryEnabled: false so the UI can *show* the user it is off').
- **Desktop shell, layout and visual design** — src/components/ModalSurface.tsx:31-32 — 'Verifying it honestly would take a driven WebView2 window, which is not available here'. False at HEAD on the platform Vela ships on: a driven WebView2 window was used and answered the question yes — 24 forward and 24 backward Tab presses stayed inside the one-stop palette, 8 stayed inside the alertdialog, and Escape restored focus to the opener rather than <body>.
- **Desktop shell, layout and visual design** — src/app/shell/TitleBar.tsx:25-26 — 'it is **last** in the tab order, so nothing tabs *through* it on the way somewhere else'. Bar-scoped, false app-scoped. The title bar is the first landmark in the document, so the measured order is Theme → Minimise → Maximise → Close → sidebar → main and a forward Tab does pass over Close. Passing over is not activating; the protection that actually carries the weight is that Close acts on a real click.
- **Desktop shell, layout and visual design** — src/features/navigation/HomeSurface.tsx:47 — 'Conversations, projects, search, tools, and a transcript…'. A user-facing claim on the screen whose own header (lines 10-13) forbids unearned impressions. At HEAD src/features/ contains attachments, canvas, conversation, diagnostics, memory, models and navigation: no projects surface, no tools/MCP surface.
- **Desktop shell, layout and visual design** — src/app/shell/AppShell.tsx:42 with src/app/shell/TitleBar.tsx:48-49 — the prop is documented as 'the open workspace' but the shell passes the constant 'Untitled workspace' in every state, and navigation-store.ts:33-45 has no workspace concept to read. The bar states something about state that nothing derives.
- **Desktop shell, layout and visual design** — src/app/shell/AppShell.tsx:63 — 'Offline · no telemetry' is rendered unconditionally, including when a remote provider endpoint is configured and in use. The 'no telemetry' half is true of the command surface (no telemetry command exists anywhere in COMMAND_ALLOWLIST — re-derived) but is asserted rather than read; the 'Offline' half is not derived from anything at all.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — src/platform/contract.ts:1692-1697 — 'The reverse direction (every command is listed) is asserted at runtime in `contract.test.ts`'. FALSE at HEAD. contract.test.ts compares COMMAND_ALLOWLIST against a hand-written array typed `(keyof IpcContract)[]`; that type requires each element to BE a key, not the list to COVER the keys. Nothing derives from IpcContract. What is actually true: the invariant is held by the exhaustive switch in src/platform/browser-adapter.ts:909 that bottoms out in `never`, and is caught only by `pnpm typecheck`, never by any test.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — src/platform/contract.test.ts:12-13 — 'Runtime exhaustiveness: a key added to IpcContract but forgotten in the allowlist would be unreachable from the renderer.' FALSE at HEAD. There is no runtime exhaustiveness here. Adding `zz_audit_probe` to IpcContract and to nothing else passes all 24 platform test files and 1187 tests, including this one and the 80-case adapter-parity suite.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — src/platform/chat-contract-parity.test.ts:372-373 — '`git config core.autocrlf` is `true` on Windows and there is no `.gitattributes`'. FALSE at HEAD: `.gitattributes` exists at repo root and carries `*.sse -text` and `*.jsonl -text`. What is actually true: it does not cover `.rs`, so model.rs/mod.rs are still CRLF in a Windows checkout (mod.rs measures CR=411) and the split(/\r?\n/) repair is still load-bearing — but the stated premise reads as if no attribute file exists, which is now wrong and would mislead anyone deciding whether to extend it.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — .gitattributes:16-18 — 'the recorded transcripts under `docs/regression-baseline/`, which `scripts/check-transcripts.sh` compares byte for byte'. Two falsehoods. (a) Coverage: counted at HEAD, docs/regression-baseline/mock-matrix holds 48 .json, 43 .txt, 12 .sse, 2 .md, 1 .tsv — the two patterns cover 12 of 106 tracked files, and the uncovered .txt/.json measure CR=7 and CR=8, i.e. still translated. (b) Mechanism: check-transcripts.sh:26 decides with `git diff --quiet -- "$evidence"`, which under core.autocrlf=true normalises line endings on read. It is not a byte comparison.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — .gitattributes:32 — repeats 'the ones `scripts/check-transcripts.sh` compares byte for byte'. Same falsehood as :18; the comparison is `git diff`, not bytes.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — .github/workflows/ci.yml:230-234 — 'The captures under `docs/regression-baseline/` are compared byte for byte. On a checkout that rewrote their line endings this reports a behaviour change nobody made, which is precisely the failure `.gitattributes` now prevents — so running it here is how we find out if that protection ever stops working.' FALSE on both counts. The comparison is `git diff --quiet`. And measured in a throwaway repo: with the attribute REMOVED, a CRLF checkout regenerated as LF gives CLEAN — the named failure mode is invisible. What is actually true: check-transcripts.sh catches only the opposite case (a rewrite while `-text` is still in force), and the real detector for a lost attribute is `cargo test --workspace`, where CRLF-ifying the seven anthropic .sse fixtures fails control_a / control_d / control_e of the replay suite.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — scripts/check-transcripts.sh:27 — prints '$evidence is byte-identical after regeneration.' FALSE for the same reason: the decision on line 26 is `git diff --quiet`, which is content-normalised. The script asserts a byte identity it never measured.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — scripts/check-transcripts.sh:12-13 — '`pnpm verify` runs it locally, and CI runs the same file.' FALSE on Windows: `pnpm run test:transcripts` dies with "'.' is not recognized as an internal or external command" because pnpm runs lifecycle scripts through cmd.exe and .npmrc sets no `script-shell`. The sentence immediately above it — 'a check that only runs in CI is a check builders discover by breaking' — is an exact description of this script's actual status on the platform Vela ships on.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — src/platform/verify-covers-ci.test.ts:85-88 — 'Both spellings: `- run: x` (a step with no name) and a `run:` line under a `- name:`. FALSE by omission: there is a third form, `run: \|`, whose body lines are never inspected (line 89's regex captures the literal `\|` and line 91 filters it). ci.yml already uses that form at lines 60 and 122. A gate written in block form escapes entirely — literally the 'whole class of step silently unchecked' the comment claims to have closed.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — src/platform/verify-covers-ci.test.ts:96 — the exemption `!line.startsWith('sudo apt-get')` implies apt-get lines reach `runLines`.
