- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/src/redact.rs:20 — 'Two mechanisms close it, and the first is the one that matters.' FALSE at HEAD. Gutting `RequestUrl::redacted()` to return the wire form left all 34 credential-canary integration tests green. What is actually true: the load-bearing mechanism is a third one these docs never mention — `ProviderError` has no field that can hold endpoint- or client-supplied text, because `map_reqwest_error` (http.rs:1174-1179) emits a typed `TransportFailure` + `Diagnosis` and routes reqwest's own string, with `.without_url()` applied, to the debug log instead.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/src/redact.rs:24-25 — 'there is exactly one production call site, in the transport' for `RequestUrl::expose`. FALSE literally: `git grep '\.url\.expose()'` returns http.rs:1039 and http.rs:1040. They are the Get and Post arms of one match, so 'one place' (the phrasing at http.rs:1036) is defensible; 'exactly one call site' is offered as a grep-ability guarantee and does not survive the grep.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — src-tauri/src/ipc/mod.rs:224-225 — 'Structural guard for the one-way rule.' FALSE. The test body at :226-230 is `assert!(!COMMAND_ALLOWLIST.contains(&"secrets_get"))`, one literal string match; `secrets_read` / `secrets_reveal` / `settings_get_credential` all pass it. What is actually true: the structural guard is `SecretValue` deriving `Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Deserialize` and NOT `Serialize` at src-tauri/crates/vela-core/src/secret.rs:39-41 — such a command would not compile. The same overstatement is mirrored at src/platform/contract.ts:31 and src/platform/contract.test.ts:85.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/location.rs:4-5 and src-tauri/crates/vela-store/src/lib.rs:25-26 — 'the Tauri host resolves the real per-user application-data directory', unqualified. True as a resolution claim, false in the security implication it carries, and it is verbatim in spirit the sentence private_fs.rs:15-19 was written to demolish. What is actually true at HEAD: location.rs:65 creates that directory with a bare `std::fs::create_dir_all`, and Get-Acl on this machine shows %APPDATA%\dev.vela.desktop\vela.db as AreAccessRulesProtected=False carrying DESKTOP-298M5DU\CodexSandboxUsers ReadAndExecute inherited=True. debuglog.rs:144-158 carries an explicit correction for the identical sentence; these two files do not.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — src/data/settings-repository.ts:9-11 — 'There is no read; the value goes to the OS keychain and the renderer only ever learns `credentialPresent: boolean` from a snapshot.' Imprecise at HEAD (minor, no security consequence): this same file exposes `hasCredential` at :46-47 / :77-80, which invokes `secrets_status` and returns presence directly. No credential VALUE crosses either way, so the security claim holds; the 'only ever ... from a snapshot' clause is wrong about what the file offers, and it happens to be right about what ships only because `hasCredential` has no product caller.
- **Providers and model layer** — src-tauri/crates/vela-providers/src/redact.rs:25 — 'there is exactly one production call site, in the transport'. False at HEAD: RequestUrl::expose has TWO production call sites, http.rs:1039 (the GET arm) and http.rs:1040 (the POST arm) of the same reqwest builder. Immaterial to the property, but the crate's own style is to make such statements greppable, and this one fails its own grep.
- **Providers and model layer** — src/features/models/capability-rows.ts:11-15 — 'Degradation must be explicit. There is no "absent" row that renders as blank and no capability that is simply not mentioned: every one of them says either what you have or what you do not have, in the same list, in the same order.' False at HEAD for two of the seven capabilities. The Thinking row is pushed only when `probed && capabilities?.reasoning === true` (:178), so a model that does not reason gets no reasoning row at all. The Token-counts row is pushed only when `probed && capabilities?.usageReporting !== true` (:189), so a model that does report usage gets no usage row. Neither ever receives the 'Not established' floor the file promises, and the list is not the same length or order across models — which is precisely the 'the affordance isn't there' failure the docblock says is not telling the user anything.
- **Providers and model layer** — src/features/models/capability-rows.ts:96-99 — the 'Tools: Emulated' row tells the user 'This endpoint has no tool calling of its own. Vela describes the tools in the prompt and reads the call back out of the reply, so a call the model gets wrong is shown to you rather than run.' False at HEAD as a description of the shipped product: on exactly the models that render this row (tool_calling == Unsupported), use-conversation.ts:610 hides the only surface that can send tools, so no catalogue is ever described and no call is ever read back. This is a false sentence shown to a user, not only to a reader.
- **Providers and model layer** — src/features/conversation/use-conversation.ts:604-611 — 'the model has to be able to request a tool: mergeRunCapabilities ANDs the harness's toolExecution with the model's toolCalls, so on a model that cannot ask for one the loop ends after a single turn and an agent run *is* a plain send — a control that would promise a fan-out it can never produce.' The description of the code is accurate; the premise is false. A model with tool_calling == Unsupported CAN be offered tools — that is what needs_tool_emulation() (capability.rs:205-207) and CompatOptions.emulate_tools: true exist for, and ModelCapabilityReport.toolCallsEmulated reports it in the very report this hook reads. The comment conflates native tool calling with 'can ask for a tool', and the gate it justifies is what makes the entire emulation subsystem unreachable.
- **Providers and model layer** — src-tauri/src/provider_host.rs:247-248 and :49 — build's doc calls itself 'The single `match`-shaped decision in the host … why every arm is the OpenAI-compatible one today', and the module doc says a protocol choice 'becomes one more arm of the `match` in ProviderHost::build'. False as written at HEAD: build (:250-262) contains no `match` and no arms — it is an unconditional `Ok(Arc::new(CompatProvider::new(..)))`. A reader who greps for the match to add an arm to finds nothing. Minor, but it is the same defect class: prose describing a structure the code does not have.
- **Providers and model layer** — docs/audit/REPORT.md:364 — grades 'Tool-call emulation for models with no native tools' as **PASS**, shipped `reaches-user`. False at HEAD, and this backlog's first item is the proof: the affordance is gated on native tool calling, so the emulation path reaches no user at all. docs/audit/providers.md:548 carries the same error in the §5 table ('Emulated tool calling \| yes, but only via the agentic runtime') — the agentic runtime is exactly where the gate is.

---

## Notes that did not fit a row (88)

- **Storage, migrations and search** — Domain totals as graded: 23 aspects, 17 PASS, 5 FAIL, 1 UNVERIFIED, 3 not-wired. Backlog items above: 8 — the 5 FAILs, the 1 UNVERIFIED, and 2 PASS rows whose `shipped` grade I re-derived as not-wired (schedules-survive-restart, default-project-seed). All three originally-marked not-wired rows were already FAILs, so there is no double-count.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — The single highest-return item is the silent startup abort. It is not just a bad error message — it is the terminal state of five other aspects in this domain. SchemaAhead, MigrationChanged, the per-migration rollback, validate_sequence and the checksum ledger are all mutation-proven, all correct, and all of them end in a user double-clicking an icon and watching nothing happen. Fixing one dialog converts five traced grades into things a critic can actually observe.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — Three gaps the auditor named that are real work but did not get a graded row, so they have no item above: (a) no test asserts FTS cleanup on a single-message delete — only the conversation-level cascade is covered, and search_messages' inner join at sqlite.rs:1116-1118 makes a miss invisible, so it would leak index rows silently; (b) store_host::database_location's %APPDATA%\\<identifier> resolution is untested because every test substitutes a tempdir, so the one path that decides where a real user's data lives has never been exercised; (c) migration 0005 stamps the seeded project's created_at/updated_at from strftime('%s','now') * 1000, bypassing the injected Clock and losing millisecond precision, pinned by no test.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — On the regrade count: 14 of the domain's 17 PASS rows drop to UNVERIFIED under the supreme rule. That is uncomfortable and it is correct — the auditor states plainly at storage.md:432 that nothing was run against the app itself and that every reaches-user grade is a traced call path. The honest read of this domain is that the Rust is genuinely excellent (7 mutations, 7 correct outcomes) and that essentially none of its user-facing consequences have been watched happen. One launch-and-click session against a real %APPDATA% database would settle nine of the fourteen.
- **Storage, migrations and search** — Cross-domain consistency to flag to the lead: this domain graded 'schedules survive a restart' as reaches-user while the Phase-3 domain graded 'schedules user surface' as FAIL/not-wired (REPORT.md:435). Both cannot be right. I confirmed the Phase-3 reading — there is no src/features/schedules/ and no schedules repository — so this domain's row is the one that needs correcting. The same shape applies to the default-project seed versus the Projects domain's 'A user-facing project surface \| FAIL \| not-wired' (REPORT.md:316).
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — Count correction before anyone quotes it: src/platform/contract.ts carries EIGHT project_* commands (:1650-1657), not nine. The lead's 'nine project_* commands' appears to fold in skills_read, which is the 'no command reads a skill's body' half of the same falsehood. The accurate line is: eight project_*, two skills_*, one mcp_list_tools, five memory_* — all fifteen registered in generate_handler! at src-tauri/src/lib.rs:193-229 and all fifteen bound by src-tauri/tests/handler_binding.rs.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — The structural reason three shipping repositories can sit off the import graph with every suite green: src/runtime/reachable.test.ts:137 walks only `shippingModules(join(SRC_ROOT, 'runtime'))`. src/data is ungoverned. Widening that walk to all of src/ is a small change that converts src/data/mcp-repository.ts, src/data/skills-repository.ts and src/data/sandbox-repository.ts from silent orphans into three named red tests. It should land BEFORE the WIRE items, so that a wiring job that gets half-finished cannot regress into the same invisible state.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — Nothing binds BrowserAdapter's ~350 lines of project validation to the host's. Re-derived at HEAD: `grep -c 'project\\|skills\\|mcp'` returns 0 for both src-tauri/tests/adapter_parity_fixture.rs and src/platform/adapter-parity.test.ts. src/platform/project-host-parity.test.ts pins only wire names, two length constants and the Windows link branch — not validation behaviour. Today this only affects `pnpm dev`; the moment a project surface exists (item 1), the dev double and the packaged app can silently disagree about which project names, working directories and skill sets are legal. Extending adapter-parity to cover the project commands belongs in the same change as item 1, not after it.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — src/platform/project-host-parity.test.ts:15-17 builds its own justification on ipc/skills.rs's comment ('src-tauri/src/ipc/skills.rs says no command calls it'). Its conclusion is true and its crate choice is right, but it inherits a false premise, so fixing the DOC item means editing this header too — otherwise the one guard that reads the live crate still explains itself with an expired reason.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — Sequencing: item 1 (project surface) is the gate. Items 2, 3, 10, 11, 12, 13 and 14 are all reachable only through it. Building any more host code first produces more excellent Rust that nobody can click, which is precisely the pattern this audit exists to stop.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — Item 4 (the false comments) is cheap and should go first anyway, because item 3's stub cites one of them as its reason. Removing the false sentence removes the argument for the stub, and the next reader of app-runtime.ts stops being told a lie that has already propagated into three other files.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — Two things in this domain need a Windows machine with a redirected or network-backed %APPDATA% before they can be believed: probe_link_strategy's Copy arm and the whole copy-materialisation path (copy_one, refresh_copy, the stale flag). It has crate tests and has never run on a machine that actually needs it. Not a backlog row because nothing is provably wrong — but it is the largest untested branch in vela-projects and it will first execute on a corporate laptop, not here.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — occupant_of's PresentButUnnamed arm is reachable only through a Windows ACL that grants traverse and withholds FILE_LIST_DIRECTORY. refusal_for's decision over the enum bites under mutation, so the decision is real, but no test constructs that ACL — windows_junction::stored_name returning Err on a real directory is asserted, not measured. Also not a row: nothing is known to be wrong, and building that ACL fixture is expensive.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — Whether vela-skills::mount has already drifted semantically from vela-projects::mount is unknown — both suites are green, neither was diffed. If they have drifted, item 6 is not merely a mislabelled guard but an actively wrong one, pinning the wrong copy to the TypeScript contract. Resolving item 5 (delete one copy) makes the question moot, which is another argument for doing 5 before 6.
- **Projects, skills and MCP** — Provenance of this backlog: I was read-only in a worktree shared with eleven other readers. I re-derived every claim above with git grep / sed against HEAD 92cdb06 and left `git status --porcelain` empty; I ran no build, no test and no app. Every acceptance test in this backlog is written as something that has not yet happened, because nothing in this domain has ever been observed in a running window — not by the original auditor, and not by me.
- **Conversation surface** — Scope note on the FAIL count: the four FAIL rows in the table become six backlog entries, because 'Tool-call surface on the ordinary chat path' is three separately actionable pieces of work — the payload cannot carry tools (chat-repository.ts:26-37 has no field), the catalogue is on no import graph (createMcpRepository has zero production callers), and the results/running render states have no caller on either path. Wiring any one of them alone still shows the user nothing.
- **Conversation surface** — Two aspects graded PASS are in the backlog because their `shipped` column is wrong in substance: 'Degradation notices' (two of eleven arms cannot fire on any path, because ChatSendReq carries no schema field) and 'Agent path: failure sentences vs failure rendering' (the evidence for the PASS is that deleting the refusal box left all 15 agent tests green). Neither is labelled not-wired or tests-only in REPORT.md, and both should be.
- **Conversation surface** — One item in the backlog is not in the audit table at all: the ordinary path's write-on-settle gap. conversation.md §8 names it and REPORT.md files it under 'could not establish', but at HEAD it is a plain not-wired: store_update_message is registered (lib.rs:239), transcript-repository exposes update() (transcript-repository.ts:64), and agent-loop-harness.ts:375-391 already uses it to open and settle a streaming row — the plain chat path calls append only, so an ordinary turn interrupted by a window close loses the user's own question. Host complete, one path already using it, the other not.
- **Conversation surface** — The 239/239 green headline for this domain is weaker than it reads. Two unmutated failures under machine contention, and with one mutation applied all 15 agent-run tests failed in a three-file parallel run versus 1 alone. Until the memory-race item is settled, treat 'the conversation suite is green' as an unproven claim about a loaded machine, not a fact.
- **Conversation surface** — Nothing in this domain can currently be exercised the way the supreme rule demands, and that is itself work someone must own: there is no route by which a conversation is driven in a real window against a real endpoint. Every claim here — including the three FAILs — is jsdom over a fake host. Until a real-window path exists, this domain's grades cannot rise above UNVERIFIED no matter how many mutations bite.
- **Conversation surface** — Two smaller unverifiables worth carrying forward but not worth their own row: CopyButton has only been tested through a stubbed navigator.clipboard, and the transcript's aria-live=polite / role=log region during a fast stream has never been heard by a screen reader — a per-token live region can be unusable and jsdom cannot tell anyone.
- **Conversation surface** — I did not modify any tracked file. Everything above was established by reading at HEAD 92cdb06 plus ripgrep over src/; no build, no test run, no app launch.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — Provenance: the audit was written at a1fa55e; HEAD is 92cdb06 and `git diff --stat a1fa55e..HEAD -- src/runtime src/features/conversation src/app src/platform` is empty. 92cdb06 added only docs. Every line number in this backlog was re-read at HEAD, not copied from the audit.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — Two distinct degradation types share a word and this will trip whoever picks up the degradations entry. `Degradation` (contract.ts:648) is provider-level, has eleven kinds, is worded by describeDegradation in src/features/conversation/notices.ts, and IS rendered — MessageTurn.tsx:75 → DegradationNotes. `RunDegradation` (contract-harness.ts:1152-1164) is run-level, has FIVE kinds, and has no wording and no renderer. The audit doc and REPORT row name four kinds; the fifth is `auxiliaryModelUnavailable`, which is correctly never emitted — agent-loop-harness.ts:548-553 explains that this loop has no internal steps so emitting it would report a reduction that did not happen. That is honest and is not backlog work.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — The renderer is closer than 'not-wired' suggests for the tool-call entry. ToolCallList already models a 'running' status (tool-calls.ts:159-166) and TurnNotices.tsx:42-53 already declares the `running` and `results` props with the right documentation. The missing work is one prop pass at MessageTurn.tsx:74 plus the two listener arms in use-conversation.ts. That makes it the cheapest high-visibility item in this domain.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — The forConversation entry and the late-join entry are one piece of work in two rows; do not staff them separately. Note also that App.tsx:119 memoises createAgentRuntime on `adapter`, so the directory genuinely survives the remount — the orphaned run is still sitting in it, addressable, ten feet from a surface that cannot see it. This is the single clearest instance in the domain of FINDING 1.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — The domain has no route to a PASS under the supreme rule without a packaged Tauri build. BrowserAdapter reports only `streaming: true` from its probe (browser-adapter.ts:1258-1263) by deliberate design, so the agent toggle cannot render in `pnpm dev` in a browser, and its #chatSend hardcodes `toolCalls: []` (browser-adapter.ts:1403), so no tool call can ever occur under the fake. Whoever verifies this domain needs the packaged build plus the tool-calling endpoint, or a seeded tool-call script on BrowserAdapter — and the second is worth building for its own sake, because it is also what would let composition-root.test.tsx cover the subagent path.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — Priority order I would defend: (1) the DOC entry, because it is a day's work and it is currently the stated justification for stubbing a feature that the host already supports; (2) readProjectInstructions, one line plus a project selector, which resurrects an entire tested layer; (3) forConversation re-attach + late join, which removes a ten-minute lockout with no cancel affordance; (4) toolCallStarted/Finished into ToolCalls; (5) degradations; (6) list()/snapshot(); (7) the contextFor memo test, which must land before or with (2) or it becomes a live cross-project leak.
- **Agentic runtime and tool execution** — One thing the audit doc understates: the acceptance for entry 1 requires rewriting an existing green test. agent-run.test.tsx:205 does not merely fail to cover re-attachment — its comment ('Not hypothetical, and this is the shape it takes in the product') and its assertion of RUN_BUSY encode the current broken behaviour as intended. Leaving it in place would make the fix look like a regression.
- **Desktop shell, layout and visual design** — Re-derivations I ran at HEAD 92cdb06 that CONFIRM the auditor: `git grep "select(null)" -- src` → exactly two hits, one non-test (use-conversations.ts:160, the delete path) and one test (navigation-store.test.ts:68). `git grep "forced-colors\\|-ms-high-contrast" -- src src-tauri` → zero matches. ConversationRow.module.css lines 12-15 and 38-40 are exactly as quoted. The palette's action array really is a single row (CommandPalette.tsx:154). The wordmark and mark really are <span>s (TitleBar.tsx:124-127).
- **Desktop shell, layout and visual design** — Tag caveat: items 4-8 and 13-14 are verification work against code believed correct, not diffs. The tag vocabulary has no 'verify' value, so they are tagged FIX. Their acceptance tests are observations in a running window — closing them with a code change and a green unit test would repeat exactly the failure this audit found.
- **Desktop shell, layout and visual design** — The design-system.test.ts evidence gap is partly closed from outside this domain: the documentation auditor added a new .module.css containing #ff00ff and var(--vela-not-a-real-token) and watched both halves fire. The scanner walks src/ recursively at test time (design-system.test.ts:34-48) with a >20-sheet floor at :70, so file discovery is live. The only untested claim left is that an *existing shipping* sheet is in that set.
- **Desktop shell, layout and visual design** — I did NOT claim provider/endpoint configuration is unreachable. src/features/models carries EndpointsPanel.tsx and EndpointForm.tsx and the auditor's 54-element enumeration saw a model picker in the content region; whether those panels are reachable belongs to the models domain's backlog, not this one.
- **Desktop shell, layout and visual design** — Item 12 (no shell-side navigation concept) is deliberately scoped to the shell: the store has no section/route, the content region is a binary switch, the sidebar has four controls and the palette one action. The repositories, IPC call sites and per-feature panels for Projects/Skills/MCP/Sandbox/Scheduler belong to those auditors' backlogs — but every one of their WIRE items needs this item done first, which is why it is marked blocking.
- **Desktop shell, layout and visual design** — Contamination carried forward from the audit session: at ~13:33 another agent ran `pnpm build` while a --vela-text-subtle mutation was live, baking night-400 into dist/. dist/ was restored byte-for-byte and re-verified, but any measurement another auditor took from dist/ between roughly 13:33 and 13:41 should be re-taken.
- **Desktop shell, layout and visual design** — I modified nothing. `git status --porcelain` was empty when I started and I ran no builds, no test runs and no app launches — only reads and read-only greps.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — Repo root for every path above: C:\Users\User\vela-tmp. Paths are repo-relative to match the audit corpus.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — HEAD moved from a1fa55e (the audit doc's stated baseline) to 92cdb06, and I verified the delta: one commit, docs/audit/* only — 13 files, 7117 insertions, zero code. `git status --short` is empty. Every code measurement in guards.md therefore applies verbatim at HEAD, and I re-derived the ones that mattered against live files rather than trusting the transcription.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — Domain shape: 23 rows — 4 FAIL, 1 UNVERIFIED, 18 PASS. Only one row carries shipped:not-wired (the `pnpm verify` row, already FAIL); none is tests-only. So the not-wired/tests-only transcription rule adds nothing here, and the real yield of this domain is the regrade list: 9 of 18 PASS rows do not survive the supreme rule.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — The `shipped` column is a category error for this domain. It reads `reaches-user` for 17 of 18 PASS rows, but a CI/test guard reaches a user only in the sense that it runs in a gate a human actually hits — and two of these provably do not: `pnpm verify` cannot execute on Windows at all, and `test-windows` has never run on a hosted runner. Recommend re-labelling this domain's column to something like gate-executes / gate-never-executed before the backlog inherits the wrong reading.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — There is one PASS row I would defend unchanged under the supreme rule: scripts/secret-scan.sh and its self-test (REPORT.md:294). It was genuinely executed on this machine — 12 passed, 0 failed — planting real key shapes in throwaway git repos, including the negative cases, including scanning the real tree and its own scanner, with excluded_paths=() empty and the no-glob rule enforced by the suite. That is a click on the running thing. Its only unproven half is that it blocks a merge in CI. Keeping exactly one survivor is the point: the other nine regrades are not a blanket downgrade.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — Three FAILs in this domain are the same defect in different clothes: a gate everyone believes runs, which does not. verify cannot execute on Windows; the accounting test cannot see block-form gates; contract.test.ts's advertised runtime check is not one. If only one thing is fixed first, fix `pnpm verify` on Windows — it is a one-line change (`script-shell` in .npmrc, or `bash ./scripts/…` in package.json) and it unblocks four of nine gates including both cargo gates for every Windows builder.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — Still not graded and still worth grading: scripts/gate-m-composition-root-controls.sh, scripts/gate-m-debug-log-modes.sh, scripts/gate-m-debug-log-acl.ps1 and src-tauri/tests/gate_m_debug_log_acl.rs. The .ps1 matters twice — it is both ungraded and the file claimed-guards structurally cannot see.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — The 58 behavioural rules of the three frozen contracts were taken as given from an earlier session's record and were not re-derived by this audit. Row 297 verified the enforcement mechanism (tsc against importers) and, per my regrade, verified it properly only for contract-sandbox.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — Calibration note on FINDING 2's arithmetic, offered so the backlog does not inherit an off-by-one from the document written to catch off-by-ones: contract.ts:1650-1657 carries EIGHT project_* commands, not nine. REPORT.md:312's 'nine' is defensible as eight project_* plus one skill-body command (skills_read at the allowlist's :1676-1677 region, alongside skills_list). The brief's phrasing 'nine project_* commands' is the imprecise one. The substance is untouched: the claim that no project command exists is false either way.
- **Guards, contracts and the verification apparatus** — I ran no builds, no app, no cargo, no pnpm verify, and touched no tracked file. All evidence above is read-only git plus file reads; the two behavioural claims I could not re-derive without running something (check-transcripts determinism, and whether src-tauri compiles at HEAD) are filed as backlog items rather than asserted.
- **Sandbox and code execution** — Sequencing. Item 'End-to-end reachability' is the gate for the whole domain: nothing else here has an acceptance test that can be executed by clicking, because there is no surface. Everything else in this backlog should be scheduled after it or alongside it, and any acceptance test written before it lands is necessarily a cargo-test acceptance test, which the supreme rule says is not enough.
- **Sandbox and code execution** — The two blocking DOC items are cheap and should go first regardless of sequencing — both understate what exists, and both are the exact failure mode FINDING 2 names: a builder who greps contract-sandbox.ts:1900 concludes the sandbox is unbuilt and builds a second path beside the one that ships.
- **Sandbox and code execution** — Uncalled-out gap from a PASS row: HostFailureReason::ScratchUnavailable and CopyOutFailed have no producers anywhere, and unlike the four producerless RefusalReason members (skillsMountMustBeReadOnly, guestPathRemapUnsupported, limitAboveHostCeiling, documentGrantInvalid — each named in the contract with a reason) this pair is called out nowhere. Small DOC job: either name them in contract-sandbox.ts's honesty note or delete them.
- **Sandbox and code execution** — Machine-dependence of the processes defect. It turns on /bin/sh being dash. WslBackend::detect() takes the first non-Docker distribution whatever it is, so a hardcoded EnforcementLevel::Kernel is a claim whose truth varies by the user's machine. Whichever way the FIX lands, report() should either derive the level from a probe or under-claim — the crate already argues for under-claiming, in the file_write_bytes comment at wsl.rs:179-182.
- **Sandbox and code execution** — Adjacent domain, same subject: REPORT.md:438 ('Canvas sandbox host is host-held, and artifact execution can be switched off', FAIL/not-wired) covers the renderer-side LocalDocumentHost stand-in, constructed at CanvasSurface.tsx:57 with no options. That is the only sandbox-shaped thing a user can currently reach, and it is held by the process it constrains. The two backlogs describe two halves of one job — an approval surface that talks to the real host — and should be planned together.
- **Sandbox and code execution** — Evidence quality worth preserving: this domain's escape battery genuinely bites. Five of eight mutations to confinement code failed immediately with well-named messages, and the pre-mount interlock at wsl.rs:217-220 (checking /proc/net/dev has exactly one interface before the first mount) is the only guard in the audit whose failure mode is damage to the user rather than exposure of the user. Nothing in this backlog should weaken those tests; the FIX items are about the four places the battery does not reach.

The single AllowOnce in the battery (line 594) is a wrong-digest negative. So the confinement guarantees are proven only on a code path the app cannot take — captured as its own FIX item above.

Fourteen commands total across the two families (8 project + 6 sandbox) is the number vela-progress.md:2948 is wrong about.

Documented as deliberate ('an empty list is never evidence that the provider is broken'), but combined with having no test at all it means a completely broken provider answers GET /v1/models with 200 and []. Worth a decision, not necessarily a change.

Ordering matters: item 6 (a switch) and item 9 (a ProviderBrain test plus one hardware turn) together convert five behind-flag rows from unreachable-and-unmeasured to shippable. Nothing else in this domain buys as much per unit of work.
- **Scheduler, dual local endpoint, memory, canvas/artifacts** — One pattern for the lead: in this domain every false comment is a stale 'X does not exist' that was true when written and became false when X was built elsewhere. The sandbox case even has the corrected text already sitting in contract-sandbox.ts:64-77 warning about exactly this. A grep-level guard — a test that fails when a comment claims a command is unallowlisted while it appears in ALLOWED_COMMANDS — would have caught four of the seven false comments here and the app-runtime.ts:20 calibration case.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — Domain shape: 20 aspects, 17 PASS / 2 FAIL / 1 UNVERIFIED / 0 not-wired. No row in this domain carries `shipped: not-wired` or `tests-only`, so the mandatory backlog was only 3 entries. The other 12 come from the audit's own 'What I could not establish' section (§11 of docs/audit/security.md, and the seven bullets under the domain in REPORT.md), from re-derivation at HEAD, and from the false-comment hunt. I judged them real work with real acceptance tests rather than padding.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — On the regrade count: 12 of 17 PASS rows. Three classes are mixed in there and they are not equally damning. (a) Traced-call-path rows — the credential write surface, secretBackend honesty, KeyringStore-is-live. These are FINDING 1 exactly and deserve the demotion. (b) Rows where the property is not clickable by nature — `SecretValue` is not Serialize, no `secrets_get` on the surface, the log cannot be read back through IPC. These are absence properties; a mutation test on real Windows hardware is the strongest evidence that exists for them, and demanding a click is a category error. I still listed the read-only ones honestly, but if the lead wants a rule that survives contact, it should say that an absence property is PASS on a mutation that bites and UNVERIFIED on inspection alone. (c) The diagnostics-DACL row, which I think is the genuinely instructive one: the evidence is a live Get-Acl reading, which feels like the strongest evidence in the whole domain, and it is a fossil left by an app launch at a different commit. Current filesystem state is not a test of current code.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — Two rows I did NOT regrade and would defend: 'Something holds the DACL going forward' (the Win32 apply() was made a no-op and 4 of 6 private_fs tests went red on real NTFS, with failure text proving %TEMP% itself hands down four foreign ACEs — the tests repair a genuinely hostile state, and ci.yml:186 runs them on windows-latest every push) and 'scripts/secret-scan.sh tripwire' (both scripts run here, the self-test is its own mutation harness, excluded_paths is empty and a test forbids globs). Everything else in the domain is weaker than these two.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — On the false-comment hunt specifically: I checked the 'no command exists for X' pattern named in the brief and found the two instances in my domain are CLEAN — src-tauri/src/ipc/diagnostics.rs:12 and src/features/diagnostics/DebugLogSwitch.tsx:11-14 both say `debuglog::enable` 'was called nowhere' / 'had no caller', in the past tense, describing the defect the switch closed. At HEAD the switch is real: EndpointsPanel.tsx:138 mounts DebugLogSwitch, src/features/diagnostics/dead-pointer.test.ts:82 asserts that mount, and diagnostics_debug_log_{get,set} are in contract.ts:1638-1639 and in COMMAND_ALLOWLIST. Those comments are accurate history, not the app-runtime.ts:20 pattern.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — Sequencing suggestion for the two blocking items. The app-data DACL fix (item 1) is architecturally awkward and should be scoped before it is estimated: `private_fs` lives in `vela-providers`, and `vela-store`'s Cargo.toml has no dependency on it, so the module is literally unreachable from `location.rs`. The two honest options are (a) harden in `src-tauri/src/store_host.rs::open` before `SqliteStore::open`, which keeps vela-store OS-agnostic and headlessly testable — the property the crate docs say is load-bearing — and also covers mcp-servers.json and skills/ if applied to the root, or (b) move `private_fs` into `vela-core` and depend on it from both. Option (a) is one call site and fixes items 1 and 5 together. Note the WAL and SHM files are created by SQLite, not by Vela, so hardening the containing DIRECTORY with inheritance disabled is what actually covers them — hardening vela.db alone would leave the 2.6 MB WAL exposed, which is where the recent conversation text actually lives.
- **Secrets, credentials and data-at-rest** — One thing the domain audit is right about and the backlog should not lose: the credential-at-rest guarantee genuinely holds. vela-settings/tests/no_plaintext_on_disk.rs drives the real service against a real SQLite file, closes it, and greps db + WAL + SHM for two canaries, with a non-vacuity control that asserts the keychain entry NAME is on disk. The DACL finding is about conversation content, not about keys. Nothing in this backlog should be read as 'API keys are sitting in a readable file'.
- **Providers and model layer** — The single largest new finding is not in the audit document: emulated tool calling is unreachable, and I derived it from HEAD rather than from the auditor's notes. The chain is capability.rs:45-47 (is_offerable = Supported\|Degraded) → capability.rs:191 (tool_calls = tool_calling.is_offerable()) → capability.rs:205-207 (needs_tool_emulation = tool_calling == Unsupported) → contract-harness.ts:292 (toolExecution = harness.toolExecution && model.toolCalls, with no reading of toolCallsEmulated) → use-conversation.ts:610 (agentAvailable) → use-conversation.ts:834/:935 (begin = agentOn ? startRun : start) → chat-repository.ts:26-37/:95-100 (the plain path has no tools field at all). ChatCapabilities has no toolCallsEmulated member, so mergeRunCapabilities structurally cannot express the case — the fix is a contract change, not a one-line gate edit.
- **Providers and model layer** — The auditor's positive findings held up under re-derivation. `git grep ResponseFormat\|response_format\|responseFormat`, `ReasoningRequest\|with_reasoning`, `Sampling\|CacheHints\|max_context_tokens` over src-tauri/src and src are all empty at HEAD, and Router/Candidate::new/RetryPolicy/.route( over src-tauri/src is empty. router.rs is 490 lines. ProviderHost::build is one unconditional constructor. Every provider-domain claim I re-checked was true except the ones listed under falseComments.
- **Providers and model layer** — provider_host.rs's Anthropic/Google disclosure is the opposite of the defect class this audit exists to catch — it names the limitation, gives the reason (ProviderConfig has no protocol field, by design), and states the shape of the fix. Do not treat that backlog item as a lie; treat it as a scoped feature with its blocker already written down. The same is true of ipc/models.rs rule 3 (dropping the adapter's note): the rule is right, and the FIX is to stop mislabelling the finding, not to start shipping endpoint-authored prose.
- **Providers and model layer** — promptCaching's documented meaning is weaker than its name — capability.rs states it means 'the endpoint accounts for cached input', not 'prompts get cached'. The live probe reported Supported off a zero-token cached-input field, which is exactly the weaker claim. Nothing is wrong; it is worth a UI label that does not overpromise if that row ever reaches a user (see the findings-not-rendered item).
- **Providers and model layer** — During the audit run one `cargo test -p vela-providers` failed spuriously with os error 32 on credential_canary-*.exe — shared-worktree contention with the other auditors, not a test failure; the immediate re-run was clean. A future reader will misread this as a real red if it is not recorded.
- **Providers and model layer** — The .gitattributes CRLF repair is holding on this Windows checkout (*.sse -text, *.jsonl -text; cat -A shows $ line ends, no ^M$), and chat-contract-parity parses 33/33 cases where 28 of 31 used to throw 'unterminated'. Nothing to do; recorded so nobody re-opens it.
- **Providers and model layer** — Two items in this backlog are blocked by others and should be scheduled after them: 'emulation never measured against a no-native-tools model' is blocked by the gate fix, and 'Anthropic/Google vendor behaviour is fixture-only' is blocked by the adapter-construction work. Both are marked non-blocking themselves because nothing else waits on them.

## Found while working Wave 1 (added 2026-08-15)

Items this plan did not contain, surfaced by critics while fixing something else. Each was
established by mutation or measurement, not by reading.

### The claim guard silently stops looking  **[BLOCKING]**

- **Where** `src/platform/claimed-guards.test.ts:520` — `` const BACKTICKED = /`([^`\n]{2,160})`/g ``
- **Wrong** The `{2,160}` floor does not match a one-character backticked token, so the global
  cursor never consumes it and every later backtick on that line pairs with the wrong partner. The
  scan then extracts the **gaps between** the real tokens. On ``…for `×`, for a `❯` beside a
  `.test.ts` name…`` it extracted `[", for a ", " beside a ", " name, or for a "]` — examining none
  of the four tokens. **566 lines across the tracked corpus carry that shape.** The upper bound has
  the same implication for a span over 160 characters. This is the guard whose own header argues a
  claimed guard is worse than a missing one; it has not been looking at 566 of them.
- **Done looks like** The cursor stays in phase across short and long tokens; a control in
  `this guard is not vacuous` fails if the floor returns; every newly-surfaced claim resolved by
  fixing the sentence or creating the thing, never by widening a route. Note `PATH_TOKEN` accepts a
  **leading dot**, so bare extensions like `` `.test.ts` `` will read as file claims the moment the
  phase is fixed — decide that deliberately rather than absorbing it into the fallout count.
- **Owner** `fix/guard-tokeniser-phase`

### `#[serde(rename = …)]` is invisible to every parity guard

- **Where** `src/platform/project-host-parity.test.ts:273`, and the same line in
  `src/platform/skill-store-parity.test.ts` — `parseRustItem` skips any line beginning `#[`
- **Wrong** Adding `#[serde(rename = "notASkillFile")]` above a variant changes the name that
  crosses the wire, and the parity guard stays **green, reproduced twice**. The attribute is
  already used in the tree at `vela-store/src/model.rs:1114`, so this is a live spelling, not a
  hypothetical. The guards read `rename_all` correctly and are blind to the per-variant override
  that beats it.
- **Done looks like** The parser reads `rename` where it already reads `rename_all`, and a mutation
  adding one to a real variant turns a named test red. Both files, since they share the line.

### A guard proven against a reconstruction of the defect, not the defect  **[PATTERN]**

Not a row to fix — a failure mode that appeared **three times in Wave 1**, in three unrelated
tracks, and was caught each time only by a critic re-deriving the proof from source bytes.

Each time, someone found a defect, wrote a guard, and proved the guard by re-creating the defect
**by hand**. The re-creation differed from the original in a way that mattered, so the mutation
went red, the guard shipped, and the guard could not see the thing it was built for.

1. **The crash-retry wrapper.** Its probe table had five rows; four reproduced. The one that did
   not was the row carrying the entire safety argument — "crash after a real failure" — which held
   only when the crash text *followed* the summary. The script's own header says that ordering
   never happens. The real ordering, failure inline then crash then no summary, was never tested,
   and against it the wrapper **exited 0 on a genuine regression**.
2. **The fatal-error chain walk.** `describe_chain` was proven by a test using a synthetic error
   whose `Display` did not embed its source. `tauri::Error::Setup` is a positional field with no
   `#[source]` whose `Display` embeds the whole inner message — so the walk is a **no-op** for the
   one error the comment names. The mutation proved the function walks, not that walking was needed.
3. **The retraction guard.** Built after a retracted claim shipped twice in the file that retracted
   it. Its needle is a single-line substring, and it was proven by reintroducing the phrase on one
   line. The real phrase had been **wrapped across a doc-comment break** by an 80-column margin:

   ```
   /// not private, **Vela would decline to start over an ACL stricter than the
   /// one it demands**. Deny entries are not exotic: they are exactly what an
   ```

   Wrapped: green 2/2. One line: red 2/2. Across history it catches **1 of 3** occurrences — the
   one already deleted. **It catches the form that was fixed and misses the form that shipped.**

**The rule.** When a guard is built from a defect, the mutation that proves it must use **the
defect's own bytes, recovered from git** — `git show <sha>:<path>` — never a retyped version.
A hand-written reproduction shares an author with the guard, and an author who mis-remembers the
defect in one place will mis-remember it in both. This is the mutation-testing analogue of a
vacuous assertion: the mutation is real, the red is real, and it establishes nothing.

Related: the vacuity class, five instances, where an assertion was satisfied by an ambient property
of the environment rather than by the thing under test.

### The capability grant is pinned by filename, not by what the window is granted  **[BLOCKING]**

- **Where** `src/app/shell/window-controls.test.tsx` (exact-set assertion), `src/platform/project-host-parity.test.ts:718-728` (prefix check), `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` → `app.security.capabilities`
- **Wrong** Both guards read **`src-tauri/capabilities/main.json`** and nothing enumerates the
  `src-tauri/capabilities/` **directory**. A critic added `capabilities/critic-probe.json`
  granting `core:window:allow-set-always-on-top` and `allow-hide` to window `main`, registered it
  in `tauri.conf.json`, and **both tests stayed green, 39/39**. The renderer's actual capability
  surface is the union of every registered file; the guards pin one filename.
- **Both halves are unguarded, verified independently.** No test enumerates that directory, and
  **no test asserts what `app.security.capabilities` contains** — `tauri.conf.json` is read by
  five files, and in `window-controls.test.tsx` it appears **only inside a comment**. So neither
  the new file nor its registration is seen.
- **And there is a false comment on top of it.** `src/app/shell/window-controls.test.tsx:343-345`
  says *"Widening the grant is still possible, but only by editing this list in the same commit,
  which is the review this file exists to force."* That is true of `main.json` and false of the
  renderer's capability surface, which is what a reader will take it to mean — the review it
  claims to force can be walked around with a second file.
- **Why it survived the track that fixed the other hole** `fix/guards-that-bite` replaced a prefix
  shape with an exact set, which closes widening **inside** `main.json`. This is the same defect
  one level up, and it is **pre-existing and identical at that branch's base** — neither caused nor
  claimed to be closed by it.
- **Done looks like** The assertion is over the **union of every capability file Tauri actually
  loads**, derived from `tauri.conf.json` rather than from a hard-coded path, so a second file is
  either counted or reported. Prove both directions: add a new capability file and watch a named
  test fail; add a permission to the existing one and watch it fail. Then check the same shape does
  not exist elsewhere — anywhere a guard reads one file from a directory the product reads whole.

### A named candidate for the load flake, rather than the hazard in general

- **Where** `src/features/models/EndpointsPanel.test.tsx` — `:144` carries `{ timeout: 10_000 }`;
  `:68`, `:93`, `:111`, `:121`, `:165`, `:175` and `:191` do comparable multi-field `user.type`
  work on the **default** timeout. `fillEndpointForm` at `:46-49` types three fields per call, and
  `user.type` simulates real typing character by character.
- **Wrong** One test in this file was given ten seconds *because* the default was too tight; its
  siblings doing the same work were not. That is a timeout that passes on an idle machine and
  fails on a busy one — which is the profile of the artefact that has been corrupting evidence all
  wave. A critic hit it here specifically, unrelated to the branch it was reviewing.
- **Why it matters more than the general hazard** "Parallel load fabricates failures" is true and
  unactionable. **A named test that fails for a knowable reason is fixable.** Every hour this
  stands, some agent spends a re-run distinguishing a real red from this one, and — the direction
  that actually costs — some vacuous guard passes its mutation proof because the machine was busy.
- **Done looks like** Measure first: run the file under artificial load and establish which tests
  actually exceed the default, rather than raising every timeout on suspicion. Then decide whether
  the fix is per-test timeouts, a file-level default, or `delay: null` on the `userEvent.setup()`
  so typing does not burn wall-clock it does not need. **Do not simply raise every timeout** —
  a suite that cannot fail on time is its own defect. If some failures survive the fix, that is a
  finding: it means there is a second cause and the hazard is not one thing.
- **Caution** This does not explain every observed instance and must not be written up as if it
  does. A separate unnamed single failure remains unattributed, and corroboration of a hazard is
  not corroboration of an instance.

### Concurrent agents collide in the session scratchpad

- **Where** the shared session scratchpad directory
- **Wrong** A critic's scratch clone was **replaced mid-run** by another agent's clone of a
  different repository state — different reflog origin, different `node_modules` shape, and a HEAD
  commit that does not exist in this object store. It detected the substitution, discarded the
  affected work and re-ran in a uniquely-named directory, but a less careful agent would have
  measured another agent's tree and reported it as its own.
- **Done looks like** Every agent brief requires a uniquely-named scratch subdirectory. Related:
  linked worktrees share one object store, and a transient `Permission denied` writing a loose
  object has already been observed under concurrent git use — `git fsck` reported no non-dangling
  problems, but the collision is real.

---

Evidence for every row is in `docs/audit/<domain>.md`; the consolidated grading table is `docs/audit/REPORT.md`.
